Ergodicity of the Anderson Φ 2 4 \Phi^{4}_{2} model
Hugo Eulry and Antoine Mouzard

Abstract
. We consider the parabolic stochastic quantization equation associated to the Φ 2 4 \Phi^{4}_{2} model on the torus in a spatial white noise environment. We study the long time behavior of this heat equation with independent multiplicative white noise and additive spacetime white noise, which is a singular SPDE in a singular environement and requires two different renormalization procedures. We prove that the solution is global in time with a strong a priori L p L^{p} bound independent of the initial data in 𝒞 − ε \mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon} for large p p . The quenched solution given the environment is shown to be an infinite dimensional Markov process which satisfies the strong Feller property. We prove exponential convergence to a unique invariant measure using a Doeblin criterion for the transition semigroup. In particular, our work is a generalization of a previous work by Tsatsoulis and Weber in a case which is not translation invariant hence the method makes no use of the reversibility of the dynamics or the explicit knowledge of the invariant measure and it is therefore in principle applicable to situations where these are not available such as the vector-valued case.



1.  Introduction

Consider the parabolic
\Phi^{4}_{2}
equation

(1.1)

{}\partial_{t}u+\mathcal{H}u+\mathbf{:}u^{3}\mathbf{:}=\sqrt{2}\zeta

on the torus
\mathbb{T}^{2}
driven by the Anderson Hamiltonian

(1.2)

\mathcal{H}=-\Delta+\xi

where
\xi
and
\zeta
are two independent white noises, respectively space and spacetime. This equation falls in the range of singular SPDEs and involves two renormalization procedures associated to each layer of randomness, respectively to define the Anderson Hamiltonian
\mathcal{H}
and the time evolution of the nonlinearity. In a previous work
[
11
]
, we obtained pathwhise local well-posedness of the equation for any initial data
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
with
\varepsilon>0
small enough as well as probabilistic global well-posedness for initial datum
u_{0}
distributed according to the invariant Gibbs measure proportional to

(1.3)

{}e^{-\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\mathbf{:}u^{4}\mathbf{:}}\mathrm{d}\mu^{\mathcal{H}}(u)

where
\mu^{\mathcal{H}}
is the law of the Anderson Gaussian Free Field associated to
\mathcal{H}
and
\mathbf{:}u^{4}\mathbf{:}
the Wick power. In this work, we continue the study of the long time behavior of the dynamic as well as the probabilistic properties of the underlying infinite dimensional stochastic process. We work on the quenched process
u(t)
in the infinite dimensional space
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
and prove that there exists a global solution that converges exponentially fast to a unique invariant measure for any initial data
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
. We do not use at any point the a priori knowledge of the invariant measure constructed in
[
11
]
.

The
\Phi_{d}^{4}
model has a long history as a toy model for Quantum Field Theory that we do not intend to explain here, see for example Glimm and Jaffe’s book
[
12
]
or the recent construction based on SPDEs of
\Phi_{3}^{4}
on the full space by Gubinelli and Hofmanová
[
13
]
and references therein. The measure was constructed for general polynomial nonlinearity with the so-called
P(\Phi)_{2}
model while the construction of the
\Phi_{3}^{4}
measure is already quite difficult, usually in the flat case of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mathbb{T}^{d}
which is invariant by translation. The construction of such measures is one of the motivation to the investigation of nonlinear heat equations with additive spacetime white noise following Parisi and Wu
[
20
]
with stochastic quantization. The idea is to consider a dynamic on an infinite dimensional space and construct the Gibbs measure
\mathcal{Z}^{-1}e^{-\mathcal{E}(u)}\mathrm{d}u
as the invariant probability measure of the equation

(1.4)

\partial_{t}u=-\nabla\mathcal{E}(u)+\sqrt{2}\mathrm{d}W_{t}

with
(W_{t})_{t\geq 0}
an infinite dimensional Brownian motion. In the case of
\Phi_{d}^{4}
on the torus
\mathbb{T}^{d}
, the energy is given by

(1.5)

\mathcal{E}(u)=\frac{\lambda}{4}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}u(x)^{4}\mathrm{d}x-\frac{m}{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}u(x)^{2}\mathrm{d}x+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}|\nabla u(x)|^{2}\mathrm{d}x

with coupling constant
\lambda
and mass
m
which yields the stochastic quantization equation

(1.6)

\partial_{t}u=\Delta u+mu-\lambda u^{3}+\sqrt{2}\zeta

with
\zeta
a spacetime white noise of parabolic spacetime Hölder regularity
-\frac{2+d}{2}-\kappa
for any
\kappa>0
, we fix
\lambda=m=1
in the following discussion. In dimension
d=1
, this equation is well-posed since
u
is of Hölder regularity
\frac{1}{2}-\kappa
thanks to Schauder estimates hence the cubic term is well-defined. In dimension
d=2
, the solution
u
should be a distribution of Hölder regularity
-\kappa<0
hence the cubic term is ill-defined and requires a renormalization procedure. The first strong solutions to this equation were obtained by Da Prato and Debussche
[
6
]
with the change of variable

(1.7)

u_{t}=\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}

with

the stationnary solution to the linear equation. Then

of Hölder regularity

is of positive regularity and each term in the equation is well-defined. They prove local well-posedness for the equation in
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
as well as invariance of the Gibbs measure which can be directly constructed with probabilistic global well-posedness. This is also the content of our previous work
[
11
]
for the model considered in this work with a general polynomial nonlinearity. In the more recent work
[
24
]
, Tsatsoulis and Weber consider the general
\Phi_{2}^{p}
equation and prove global well-posedness in
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
using a coming down from infinity bound uniform in the initial data. They prove that the solution is a Markov process in
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
satisfying the strong Feller property. They finally prove a support theorem for the laws of the solutions in the cubic case which implies exponential convergence to a unique invariant measure with Doeblin criterion. In particular, this achieves a construction of the
\Phi_{2}^{4}
measure using only the dynamic. In three dimensions,
is of Hölder regularity
-\frac{1}{2}-\kappa
hence the Wick products

and

are of respective Hölder regularity
-1-\kappa
and
-\frac{3}{2}-\kappa
. This implies that the solution
v
is a priori of regularity
\frac{1}{2}-\kappa
making the product
v^{2}\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}
and
v\mathbf{:}\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}^{2}\mathbf{:}
singular. This equation thus falls in the range of singular SPDE. Catellier and Chouk
[
4
]
proved local well-posedness using paracontrolled calculus following Gubinelli, Imkeller and Perkowski
[
14
]
. Mourat and Weber
[
18
]
proved a similar a priori bound in this setting which implies global well-posedness in
\mathcal{C}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}
. We finally mention the recent work by Jagannath and Perkowski
[
16
]
which relies on an exponential transform to prove global well-posedness for the equation on
v
, see also Bailleul, Dang, Ferdinand, and Tô
[
2
]
on compact manifolds in three dimensions, in particular not invariant by translation.

In this work, we are interested in the case where the Laplacian is replaced by the Anderson Hamiltonian
\mathcal{H}
with the random environment
\xi
a space white noise, which can appear as the scaling limit of Ising model with an i.i.d. environment. The operator itself is a singular stochastic operator that has to be defined with a renormalization procedure as

(1.9)

\mathcal{H}:=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}(-\Delta+\xi_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon})

with
\xi_{\varepsilon}
a regularization of the noise and
c_{\varepsilon}
a logarithmic diverging quantity. The first result of this work is an improvement from probabilistic global well-posedness to deterministic global well-posedness in negative regularity Hölder spaces
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
for
\varepsilon>0
. This is done using a priori estimates on the
L^{p}
norm of the solution for
p
large enough such that
L^{p}
is embbeded in
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
as in the usual coming down from infinity argument. The difficulty here is similar to
\Phi_{3}^{4}
since the solution
u
is paracontrolled which makes the a priori estimate hard to get. One first follows Da Prato and Debussche
[
6
]
and consider

(1.10)

u_{t}=\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(t)+v_{t}

with
\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(t)=\int_{-\infty}^{t}e^{-(t-s)\mathcal{H}}\zeta(\mathrm{d}s)
the stochastic convolution associated to the Anderson Hamiltonian. The equation on
v
is singular and we use the paracontrolled expansion. Following the construction of the Anderson Hamiltonian from
[
19
]
, the solution
v
is of the form

(1.11)

v=v\mathbin{\lx@scalerel@obj{\hbox to8.9pt{\vbox to8.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.45pt\lower-4.45pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.88 0 C 5.88 3.25 3.25 5.88 0 5.88 C -3.25 5.88 -5.88 3.25 -5.88 0 C -5.88 -3.25 -3.25 -5.88 0 -5.88 C 3.25 -5.88 5.88 -3.25 5.88 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.125pt}{-1.75pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -4.32 -2.42)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}X+w

with
X=\Delta^{-1}\xi
and
w
a smoother remainder. Up to a truncation of the low frequencies of
X
, the map
\Phi(v)=v-v\mathbin{\lx@scalerel@obj{\hbox to8.9pt{\vbox to8.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.45pt\lower-4.45pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.88 0 C 5.88 3.25 3.25 5.88 0 5.88 C -3.25 5.88 -5.88 3.25 -5.88 0 C -5.88 -3.25 -3.25 -5.88 0 -5.88 C 3.25 -5.88 5.88 -3.25 5.88 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.125pt}{-1.75pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -4.32 -2.42)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}X
which associates to
v
its remainder
w
is invertible which allows to consider the change of variable
v=\Gamma w
with
\Gamma=\Phi^{-1}
. This map has been an important tool in recent works on evolution PDEs associated to the Anderson Hamiltonian, see
[
11
,
15
]
and references therein. At the level of the equation
w
, we obtain a priori estimates on its
L^{p}
norm which allows to control the initial solution
v=\Gamma w
and prove global well-posedness, see Corollary
3.15
. This is close in spirit to the coming down from infinity argument for
\Phi_{3}^{4}
which is described by a singular SPDEs, see Mourrat and Weber
[
18
]
. This is enough to obtain existence of invariant measures in Proposition
4.5
with Krylov-Bogoliubov Theorem, this is the approach in
[
2
]
on compact surfaces in three dimensions.

We then study probabilistic properties of the solution as a stochastic process in infinite dimension. The main object of interest is the transition semigroup
(P_{t})_{t\geq 0}
defined by

(1.12)

{}P_{t}\varphi(u_{0}):=\mathbb{E}\big[\varphi(u(t\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0}))\big]

where
\varphi
is a bounded Borelian function on
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
and
u(t\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})
denotes the solution of
1.1
at time
t
starting from initial data
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
. We prove that this is a Markov semigroup satisfying the strong Feller property, that is
P_{t}\varphi
is a continuous function of
u_{0}
for
t>0
and
\varphi
a bounded Borelian function. This is done with a Bismut-Elworthy-Li formula for the semigroup to obtain a uniform bound on
\mathrm{d}(P_{t}\varphi)
which implies Lipchitz continuity for
P_{t}\varphi
. Finally, we prove exponential mixing to a unique invariant measure in Corollary
5.4
which is the main result of our work. We do not have a strong support theorem for the enhanced noise as obtained in
[
24
]
due to the roughness of the environment however we prove that it contains zero. This is enough to prove that the solution relaxes to any small ball around zero with positive probability which implies that
0
is in the support of any invariant measure hence uniqueness with the strong Feller property. In particular, this complete the case of usual
\Phi_{2}^{p}
model for general even integer
p\geq 4
in the work of Tsatsoulis and Weber
[
24
]
as they only restrict to the cubic model for the support theorem. Our method allows to consider models not invariant by translation such as similar model on any compact surfaces, we restrict to the torus for the sake of simplicity as the invariance is already lost with the Anderson Hamiltonian.

In Section
2
, we recall the renormalization result from our previous work
[
11
]
. We also improve the time regularity of the Wick power and prove that zero is in the support of the enhanced noise. In Section
3
, we first prove a general local well-posedness result in the same spirit as
[
11
]
. We then prove an a priori estimate in
L^{p}
for
p
large enough on the solution independent of
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
which implies global well-posedness. The Markov and strong Feller properties are proved in Section
4
via a Bismut-Elworthy-Li formula. In Section
5
, we prove that the solution relaxes to
0
with positive probability which implies exponential convergence to a unique invariant measure using a Doeblin criterion for the transition semigroup.

Acknoledgments :
The authors warmly thank Arnaud Debussche for fruitful and motivating dicussions about the present work. The first author was supported by the ANR projet Smooth “ANR-22-CE40-0017”.

2.  Renormalization and noise regularity

In this section, we gather all the needed results on the renormalization of the enhanced noise that will be needed. The stochastic convolution

(2.1)

\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{\infty,t}=\sqrt{2}\int_{-\infty}^{t}e^{-(t-s)\mathcal{H}}\zeta(\mathrm{d}s)

with
-\mathcal{H}
instead of the Laplacian is a stationnary Gaussian process with explicit spacetime covariance given by the Anderson heat kernel. Its law at any fixed
t\in\mathbb{R}
is given by the Anderson Gaussian free field
\mu^{\mathcal{H}}
thus of Hölder spatial regularity
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
for any
\varepsilon>0
, see Section
5
in
[
3
]
. The renormalization of its Wick power follows from estimates on the functionnal calculus associated to
\mathcal{H}
, we refer to Section
2
in
[
11
]
for details. The Wick power are defined as

\mathbf{:}\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

To study the probabilistic properties of the solution, we will also need the more general process

\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{s,t}=\sqrt{2}\int_{s}^{t}e^{-(t-r)\mathcal{H}}\zeta(\mathrm{d}r)

for any

. Since we have

\mathbf{z}_{s,t}
the enhanced noise on the time interval
[s,t]
for any

defined as
\mathbf{z}
with
\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{s,t}
instead of
. The following proposition guarantees that the noise is invariant by translation in law. It follows directly from the same property for the regularized data
\mathbf{z}_{N}
with the convergence in
L^{p}(\Omega)
.

Lemma 2.3 –

For any
t\in\mathbb{R}
, the process
\mathbf{z}_{t,t+\cdot}
and
\mathbf{z}
have the same law.

For the model with the usual Laplacian instead of the Anderson Hamitlonian
\mathcal{H}
, one can prove that the Wick powers converge in
C([0,T],\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon})
hence continuity in time follows directly. This is not known a priori in our case, we prove the continuity in time directly using Kolmogorov’s criterion. The explosion at time zero is due to the initial condition
\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(0)=0
.

Proposition 2.4 –

The enhanced noise
\mathbf{z}=(\mathbf{z}^{(1)},\mathbf{z}^{(2)},\mathbf{z}^{(3)})
belongs to
C\big((0,T],(\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa})^{3}\big)
for any
T>0
and
\kappa>0
.

Proof :

We first prove the continuity in time of the stationnary process

(2.14)

\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{\infty,t}=\sqrt{2}\int_{-\infty}^{t}e^{-(t-s)\mathcal{H}}\zeta(\mathrm{d}s)

which is a centered Gaussian process with covariance

(2.15)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{\infty,t}(x)\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{\infty,s}(y)\big]

\displaystyle=2\int_{-\infty}^{t}\int_{-\infty}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}}K_{t-t^{\prime}}(x,x^{\prime})K_{s-s^{\prime}}(y,y^{\prime})\mathbb{E}\big[\zeta(t^{\prime},x^{\prime})\zeta(s^{\prime},y^{\prime})\big]\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}

(2.16)

\displaystyle=2\int_{-\infty}^{t\wedge s}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}K_{t-u}(x,z)K_{s-u}(y,z)\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}u

(2.17)

\displaystyle=2\int_{-\infty}^{t\wedge s}K_{t+s-2u}(x,y)\mathrm{d}u

where
K_{t}(x,y)
denotes the kernel of the heat semigroup
e^{-t\mathcal{H}}
. We denote as
\Delta_{n}
the Paley-Littlewood projector with associated kernel
\chi_{n}=2^{2n}\chi(2^{n}\cdot)
, see Appendix
B
. For any random distribution
f
, we have

(2.18)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}}^{p}\big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\big[\|f\|_{B_{p,p}^{\alpha+\frac{2}{p}}}^{p}\big]

(2.19)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geq 0}2^{\alpha np}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\Delta_{n}f\|_{L^{p}}^{p}\big]

(2.20)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{n\geq 0}2^{\alpha np}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\big[|\Delta_{n}f(x)|^{p}\big]\mathrm{d}x

hence a pointwise bound on
\mathbb{E}\big[|\Delta_{n}f(x)|^{p}\big]
is enough to get Hölder regularity. We have

(2.21)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[|\Delta_{n}\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{\infty,t}(x)|^{2}\big]

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}}\chi_{n}(x-y)\chi_{n}(x-y^{\prime})\mathbb{E}\big[\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{\infty,t}(y)\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{\infty,t}(y^{\prime})\big]\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}

(2.22)

\displaystyle=2\int_{-\infty}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}}\chi_{n}(x-y)\chi_{n}(x-y^{\prime})K_{2t-2u}(y,y^{\prime})\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}\mathrm{d}u

(2.23)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}}\chi_{n}(x-y)\chi_{n}(x-y^{\prime})G(y,y^{\prime})\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}

(2.24)

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}}\chi_{n}(x-y)\chi_{n}(x-y^{\prime})\log(|y-y^{\prime}|)\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}

(2.25)

\displaystyle\lesssim\log(n)

using the support of
\chi_{n}
where
G
is the Green function of
\mathcal{H}
. For
p\geq 2
, Gaussian hypercontractivity yields

(2.26)

\mathbb{E}\big[|\Delta_{n}\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{\infty,t}(x)|^{p}\big]\lesssim\log(n)^{\frac{p}{2}}

which gives the Hölder spatial regularity
\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}
for
\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{\infty,t}
with fixed
t\in\mathbb{R}
and any
\kappa>0
. For the time regularity, we have

(2.27)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[

, we use the binomial expression

\mathbf{:}\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}

with Schauder estimates which gives an explosion as

\square

The continuity in time is not important for global well-posedness, it is however needed to prove the next proposition. In
[
24
]
, Tsatsoulis and Weber proved that the enhanced noise for the
\Phi_{2}^{4}
model on the torus
\mathbb{T}^{2}
contains
(0,a,0)
in its support for any
a\geq 0
. Their proof relies strongly on the explicit spectral theory of the Laplacian, it is not clear how to prove such a result in general including for example the case of a general compact surface or more general power. In the last section, we prove that this weak support result is enough to get uniqueness of the invariant measure with exponential ergodicity.

Proposition 2.5 –

For any
T>t_{0}>0
, we have

(2.36)

\mathbb{P}\Big(\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\|\mathbf{z}(t)\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa})^{3}}\leq\varepsilon\Big)>0

for any
\varepsilon>0
. In particular,
(0,0,0)
is in the support of
\mathbf{z}
as a random variable in
C([t_{0},T],(\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa})^{3})
.

Proof :

Our strategy of proof is to consider the real random variable

(2.37)

Z=\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\big(\|\mathbf{z}_{t}^{(1)}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}}+\|\mathbf{z}_{t}^{(2)}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}}+\|\mathbf{z}_{t}^{(3)}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}}\big)

and prove that

(2.38)

\mathbb{P}(Z<\varepsilon)>0

for any
\varepsilon>0
. This implies that
0
lies in the support of
Z
and gives the result. As done by Stroock and Varadhan
[
22
]
, it is enough to prove that

(2.39)

\mathbb{P}(Z<\varepsilon|\mathcal{A})>0

for any event
\mathcal{A}
of positive probability. The conditional Markov inequality

(2.40)

\mathbb{P}(Z<\varepsilon|\mathcal{A})\geq 1-\frac{\mathbb{E}[Z|\mathcal{A}]}{\varepsilon}

gives that the result holds if we construct an event
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{A}_{\varepsilon}
such that
\mathbb{E}[Z|\mathcal{A}]<\varepsilon
. We consider the representation of
\mathbf{z}
associated to

(2.41)

\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{\infty,t}=\sum_{n\geq 0}X_{n}(t)e_{n}

with
(X_{n})_{n\geq 0}
a family of independent stationnary Ornstein-Uhlenbeck process of parameters
(\lambda_{n})_{n\geq 0}
the eigenvalues of
\mathcal{H}
. Given any
N\geq 0
, the event

(2.42)

\mathcal{A}_{N}:=\Big\{\forall i\in\llbracket 0,N\rrbracket,\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|X_{i}(t)|\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{(N+1)\sup_{0\leq n\leq N}\|e_{n}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\kappa}}}\Big\}

is indeed of positive probability. Then we consider the decomposition

(2.43)

\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{\infty,t}=\sum_{n=0}^{N}X_{n}(t)e_{n}+\sum_{n>N}X_{n}(t)e_{n}=X_{\leq N}(t)+X_{>N}(t)

which gives for the Wick powers

(2.44)

\mathbf{:}\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{\infty,t}^{2}\mathbf{:}=X_{\leq N}(t)+2X_{\leq N}(t)X_{>N}(t)+\mathbf{:}X_{>N}(t)^{2}\mathbf{:}

and

(2.45)

\mathbf{:}\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{\infty,t}^{3}\mathbf{:}=X_{\leq N}(t)^{3}+3X_{\leq N}(t)^{2}X_{>N}(t)+3X_{\leq N}(t)\mathbf{:}X_{>N}(t)^{2}\mathbf{:}+\mathbf{:}X_{>N}(t)^{3}\mathbf{:}

for any
t\in[0,T]
. We get

(2.46)

\displaystyle\|\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{\infty,t}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}}

\displaystyle\leq\|X_{\leq N}(t)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\kappa}}+\|X_{>N}(t)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}}

(2.47)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon+\|X_{>N}(t)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}}

as well as

(2.48)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{:}\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope }

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{2}+2\varepsilon\|X_{>N}(t)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}}+\|\mathbf{:}X_{>N}(t)^{2}\mathbf{:}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}}

and

(2.49)

\|\mathbf{:}\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}

for the Wick powers. Since the random variables
\mathbf{:}X_{>N}(t)^{k}\mathbf{:}
depends only on
X_{i}
for
i>N
, we have

(2.50)

\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\mathbf{:}X_{>N}(t)^{k}\mathbf{:}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}}\big|\mathcal{A}_{N}\Big]=\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\mathbf{:}X_{>N}(t)^{k}\mathbf{:}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}}\Big]

for any
k\in\{1,2,3\}
. Following the proof of the previous proposition, we have

(2.51)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\mathbf{:}X_{>N}(t)^{k}\mathbf{:}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}}\Big]=0

using the quantitative estimate from Kolmogorov’s continuity criterion, see for example Theorem
3.4.16
from
[
21
]
. Taking
N=N_{\varepsilon}>0
large enough depending on
\varepsilon
, we get that

(2.52)

\mathbb{E}[Z|\mathcal{A}_{N}]\leq 2\varepsilon+3\varepsilon^{2}+\varepsilon+4\varepsilon^{3}+3\varepsilon^{2}+\varepsilon

hence

(2.53)

\mathbb{P}\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big(\|\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope }

for any

. Again, the result for the enhanced data
\mathbf{z}
follows from the binomial expression

(2.54)

\mathbf{:}\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}

with Schauder estimates which gives an explosion as
t
goes to zero.

\square

Remark 2.6 –

In
[
5
,
24
]
, the authors make strong use of the spectral theory of the Laplacian on the torus to construct a sequence
h_{n}
in the Cameron-Martin space that contains nonzero elements. This allows to give a strong support theorem for the enhanced noise thus for solutions of singular SPDEs. While we believe that this approach can be adapted for any potential of positive Hölder regularity, this seems out of reach in the case of the Anderson Hamiltonian or even for the Laplacian of general compact surfaces. However, in our previous work
[
11
]
, we constructed the Gibbs measure
\nu^{\mathcal{H}}
associated to the Anderson
P(\Phi_{2})
model as an absolutetly continuous measure with respect to the Anderson Gaussian free field
\mu^{\mathcal{H}}
. Since the Gaussian measure
\mu^{\mathcal{H}}
is known to be of full support in
\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}
as its Cameron-Martin space is dense, this is also the case for
\nu^{\mathcal{H}}
. We do not use this argument in the present work, which does not rely on a direct construction of the measure.

3.  General well-posedness theory

In this section, we study the truncated dynamic

(3.1)

{}\partial_{t}u_{N}+\mathcal{H}u_{N}+\mathbf{:}u_{N}^{3}\mathbf{:}=\sqrt{2}\Pi_{N}\zeta,\\

with initial data
u_{N}(0)=u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
and follow the usual Da Prato-Debussche trick from
[
6
]
in the context of the usual
\Phi_{2}^{4}
equation. We split the equation into a linear dynamics driven by
\zeta

Then
v_{N}
solves the nonlinear equation

(3.5)

{}\partial_{t}v_{N}+\mathcal{H}v_{N}+v_{N}^{3}+v_{N}^{2}\mathbf{z}_{N}^{(1)}+v_{N}\mathbf{z}_{N}^{(2)}+\mathbf{z}_{N}^{(3)}=0

with the same initial data
v_{N}(0)=u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
. Provided we can solve (
3.5
) in an appropriate functional space, solutions to (
3.1
) will therefore be constructed as
u_{N}=\Pi_{N}\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}+v_{N}
. Given the Anderson Hamiltonian
\mathcal{H}
, the equation is singular due to the irregularity of the spacetime white noise
\zeta
and the cubic nonlinearity. To construct solutions, we consider here a regularization of the stochastic forcing while a regularization of the nonlinearity was used in our previous work
[
11
]
. This choice was motivated there by our goal to prove invariance of the constructed Gibbs measure and obtain probabilistic global well-posedness while it is more convenient to work with a truncated spacetime white noise to obtain the strong Feller property. We start by proving local well-posedness within this framework with the same type or argument as in
[
11
]
. We then prove global well-posedness for any initial data in negative Hölder spaces
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
using a priori estimates.

3.1.  Local theory

We investigate the local well-posedness of (
3.1
) for initial data
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
with the change of variable

u_{N}=\Pi_{N}\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}+v_{N}

where
v_{N}
satisfies equation (
3.5
). In view of the Schauder estimate for
\mathcal{H}
from Proposition
A.2
, the solution
v_{N}
is expected to belong to the space

(3.6)

\displaystyle X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}=C([0,T],\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon})\cap C((0,T],\mathcal{C}^{\sigma})

for
\varepsilon<\sigma<1
endowed with the norm

\|v\|_{X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}}=\|v\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}+\sup_{0<t\leq T}t^{\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}}\|v(t)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}}

that takes account of the blow-up at
t=0
in
\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}
. The unusual restriction
\sigma<1
instead of
\sigma<2
in the case of the standard Laplace operator is due to the roughness of the Anderson Hamiltonian. One can prove that the solution belongs to the
L^{2}
-based Sobolev space
\mathcal{D}^{2-\kappa}
associated to
\mathcal{H}
which only embeds in
\mathcal{C}^{1-\kappa}
with
\kappa>0
, this structure will be important to obtain a priori estimates. Note that even if (
3.5
) is a truncated equation, by setting

\mathbf{f}(v,\mathbf{y}):=v^{3}+v^{2}\mathbf{y}^{(1)}+v\mathbf{y}^{(2)}+\mathbf{y}^{(3)},

it falls into the scope of the following more general problem

(3.7)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial_{t}v+\mathcal{H}v+\mathbf{f}(v,\mathbf{y})=0,\\
v(0)=v_{0},\end{cases}

for a given vector
\mathbf{y}\in L^{q}([0;T];\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon})^{3}
and initial condition
v_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
. We then have the following local well-posedness result. In particular, the convergence of
v_{N}
to a limit solution
v
follows from the convergence of
\mathbf{z}_{N}
to
\mathbf{z}
since the solution depends continuously on the data.

Proposition 3.1 –

Let
0<\varepsilon<\sigma<1
and
1\leq q<\infty
be such that
\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}\frac{q}{q-1}<\frac{1}{3}
. Then there exists
\theta,C>0
such that for any
r,R>0
, there exists
T>0
of order
\frac{r^{\theta}}{(1+r+R)^{3\theta}}
such that for any
v_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
and
\mathbf{y}\in L^{q}([0;T],\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon})^{3}
satisfying
\|v_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}\leq r
and
\|\mathbf{y}\|_{L^{q}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}\leq R
, there exists a unique solution
v
in
X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}
to (
3.7
).
Moreover, we have

(3.8)

\displaystyle\|v\|_{X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}}\leq 2Cr.

and
v
depends Lipschitz continuously on both
v_{0}
and
\mathbf{y}
.

Proof :

Given the data
(v_{0},\mathbf{y})
, we define the solution mapping
\Phi
by

\Phi(v)(t):=e^{-t\mathcal{H}}v_{0}-\int_{0}^{t}e^{-(t-s)\mathcal{H}}\mathbf{f}(v,\mathbf{y})(s)\mathrm{d}s

and seek for a solution
v
as a fixed point of
\Phi
in the ball of radius
2Cr
in
X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}
for some
C>0
and
T>0
small enough depending on
r,R
as in the statement of Proposition
3.1
. Let
v
be in the ball of radius
2Cr
in
X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}
. For the norm of negative regularity, we have

\displaystyle\big\|\Phi(v)

\displaystyle\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}\lesssim\|v_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}+\int_{0}^{T}\big\|\mathbf{f}(v,\mathbf{y})(s)\big\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\|v_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\int_{0}^{T}\|v(s)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}}^{j}\|\mathbf{y}^{(3-j)}(s)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{T}\|\mathbf{y}^{(3)}(s)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\|v_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\|v\|_{X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}}^{j}\|\mathbf{y}^{(3-j)}\|_{L^{q}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}s^{-\big(\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}\big)jq^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}}+T^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}}\|\mathbf{y}^{(3)}\|_{L^{q}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\leq Cr+C^{\prime}(1+r+R)^{3}T^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}-3\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}}

with
C,C^{\prime}>0
independant of
r
,
R
, and
T
using the expression of
\mathbf{f}
, Schauder estimate for
\mathcal{H}
and a repeated use of the product estimates in Lemma
B.1
. Thus, taking
T
of order
\big(\frac{r}{(1+r+R)^{3}}\big)^{\theta}
with
\theta=\big(\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}-3\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}\big)^{-1}>0
, we get

\displaystyle\big\|\Phi(v)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}\leq 2Cr.

Similarly as above, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{0<t\leq T}t^{\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}}

\displaystyle\big\|\Phi(v)(t)\big\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}}\lesssim\|v_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}+\sup_{0<t\leq T}t^{\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}}\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}}\|\mathbf{y}^{(3)}(s)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\sup_{0<t\leq T}t^{\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}}\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}}\|v^{j}(s)\mathbf{y}^{(3-j)}(s)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\|v_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}+\Big\{\|\mathbf{y}^{(3)}\|_{L^{q}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}\sup_{0<t\leq T}t^{\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}q^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\|v\|_{X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}}^{j}\|\mathbf{y}^{(3-j)}\|_{L^{q}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}\sup_{0<t\leq T}t^{\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}q^{\prime}}s^{-\big(\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}\big)jq^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq Cr+C^{\prime}(1+r+R)^{3}T^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}-3\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}}

where we used that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-a}s^{-b}\mathrm{d}s\lesssim t^{1-a-b}

for any
0<a,b<1
with
a+b>1
.
Putting the two estimates together with our choice of
T
yields

\displaystyle\big\|\Phi(v)\big\|_{X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}}\leq 2Cr

hence the ball of radius
2Cr
in
X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}
is stable under the map
\Phi
up to the time continuity of
\Phi(v)
. For this point, consider
0<t\leq T
and
h
small enough then split the integral as

\displaystyle\Phi(v)(t+h)-\Phi(v)(t)

\displaystyle=e^{-t\mathcal{H}}(e^{-h\mathcal{H}}-1)v_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-(t-s)\mathcal{H}}(e^{-h\mathcal{H}}-1)\mathbf{f}(v,\mathbf{y})(s)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{t}^{t+h}e^{-(t+h-s)\mathcal{H}}\mathbf{f}(v,\mathbf{y})(s)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=:(1)+(2)+(3).

We use both the Schauder estimate
A.2
and the continuity of the semigroup given by Proposition
A.1
to prove the convergence of each term as
h
goes to
0
. The first term converges to
0
by continuity of the semigroup in
\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}
. For the second term, Schauder estimate for
\mathcal{H}
gives

(3.9)

\left\|(2)\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}}\|(e^{-h\mathcal{H}}-1)\mathbf{f}(v,\mathbf{y})(s)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}\mathrm{d}s

which again converges to
0
with the dominated convergence theorem and continuity of the semigroup. The third term is controlled a similar argument, changing variables and proceeding as above we get

\displaystyle\left\|(3)\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{t}^{t+h}(t+h-s)^{-\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}}\|\mathbf{f}(v,\mathbf{y})(s)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{h}\tau^{-\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}}\|\mathbf{f}(v,\mathbf{y})(t+h-\tau)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int_{0}^{h}\tau^{-q^{\prime}\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}}(t+h-s)^{-q^{\prime}3\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}}\|v\|_{X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}}^{3}\|\mathbf{y}\|_{L^{q}_{T_{0}}\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{-3\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}}h^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}-\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}}\|v\|_{X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}}^{3}\|\mathbf{y}\|_{L^{q}_{T_{0}}\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}

which converges to
0
as
h
goes to
0
. This proves continuity of
\Phi(v)
as a
\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}
-valued map on
(0,T]
. The continuity with values in
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
at
0
is obtained via the same arguments using that
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
. Altogether, this shows that
\Phi
maps the ball of radius
2Cr
of
X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}
to itself. Similar computations as above yield

\displaystyle\big\|\Phi(v_{1})-\Phi(v_{2})\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}\leq C^{\prime}(1+r+R)^{2}T^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}-\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}3}\|v_{1}-v_{2}\|_{X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}}

and

\displaystyle\sup_{0<t\leq T}t^{\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}}\big\|\Phi(v_{1})(t)-\Phi(v_{2})(t)\big\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}}\leq C^{\prime}(1+r+R)^{2}T^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}-\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}3}\|v_{1}-v_{2}\|_{X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}}

for
v_{1},v_{2}
in the ball of radius
2Cr
of
X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}
. With our choice of
T
, this shows that
\Phi
is also a contraction on this ball, thus admits a unique fixed point
v
which is then a mild solution to (
3.7
) in
X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}
in the ball of radius
2Cr
. Given different data
(v_{0},\mathbf{y}),(\widetilde{v_{0}},\widetilde{\mathbf{y}})
, similar estimates for solutions
v,\widetilde{v}
yield

\displaystyle\|v-\widetilde{v}\|_{X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|v_{0}-\widetilde{v_{0}}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}+C^{\prime}(1+r+R)^{2}T^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}-3\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}}\|v-\widetilde{v}\|_{X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}}

\displaystyle\qquad+C^{\prime}(1+r)^{2}T^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}-3\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}}\|\mathbf{y}-\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}\|_{L^{q}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}

hence the Lipschitz dependence on
(v_{0},\mathbf{y})
of the solution.

\square

The previous proposition holds for very general choice of forcing
\mathbf{y}\in L^{q}([0;T],\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon})^{3}
. If we return to the case of interest
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{z}
, this ensures that equation (
1.1
) is locally well-posed in
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
with solution in
X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}
. Moreover, the convergenc of the data
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{z}_{N}
to
\mathbf{z}
in the correct topology ensures the convergence of
v_{N}
to
v
in
X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}
as
N
goes to infinity. It yields to following well-posedness result for the initial equation

(3.10)

\partial_{t}+\mathcal{H}u+\mathbf{:}u^{3}\mathbf{:}=\sqrt{2}\zeta

in the space

. In particular, the renormalized product
\mathbf{:}u^{3}\mathbf{:}
is defined as

and

regular enough for the different products to make sense.

Theorem 3.2 –

Let
0<\varepsilon<\frac{1}{3}
,
\varepsilon<\sigma<1
, and
q>1
be such that
\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}\frac{q}{q-1}<\frac{1}{3}
. Then
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely, for any
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
, there exists
T>0
such that for any
N\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, (
3.1
) admits a solution
u_{N}\in C([0;T];\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon})
, unique in the affine space
\Pi_{N}\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }

. Moreover

converges almost surely to a limit
u\in\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }

which is the unique solution in the class
\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}+X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}
to equation (
1.1
).

Proof :

Let
\varepsilon,\sigma,q
be as in the statement of Theorem
3.2
, and recall that
\mathbf{z}
is the enhanced data and
\mathbf{z}_{N}
its approximation. Fix
T>0
and let
R>0
. For any
N\geq 1
and
1\leq k\leq 3
, consider the event

(3.12)

\Sigma_{R}^{N,k}:=\Big\{\omega\in\Omega\ ;\ \|\mathbf{z}_{N}^{(k)}-\mathbf{z}^{(k)}\|_{L^{q}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}\leq N^{-\frac{\kappa}{2}}R,\ \|\mathbf{z}_{N}^{(k)}\|_{L^{q}_{T}\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}\leq R\Big\}

where
\kappa
is as in Proposition
2.1
. Then set

\Sigma_{R}:=\bigcap_{N\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}\bigcap_{1\leq k\leq 3}\Sigma_{R}^{N,k}

and

\Sigma:=\liminf_{R\to+\infty}\Sigma_{R}.

Using the bounds from Proposition
2.1
, we get

\mathbb{P}\left(\Omega\setminus\Sigma_{R}\right)\leq Ce^{-c\sqrt{R}}

for some constants
c,C>0
thus Borel-Cantelli Lemma gives that
\Sigma
is of full probability. Given
R>0
, we get from Proposition
3.1
that there exists a time
T=T(\|u_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}},R)>0
such that for any
\omega\in\Sigma_{R}
and
N\geq 1
, equation (
3.7
) admits a unique solution
v_{N}\in X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}
with data
v_{N}(0)=u_{0}
. We then obtain a unique solution
u_{N}
to (
3.1
) by setting

u_{N}=\Pi_{N}\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}+v_{N}\in\Pi_{N}\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}+X_{T}^{-\sigma,\varepsilon}.

Moreover since
\mathbf{z}_{N}^{(k)}\to\mathbf{z}^{(k)}
in
L^{q}([0;T_{0}];\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon})
, the Lipschitz continuity property of the solution in Proposition
3.1
ensures that
v_{N}
converges in
X_{T}^{-\varepsilon,\sigma}
to the solution
v
to (
3.7
) with data
(u_{0},\mathbf{z})
hence
u_{N}
converges to a limit
