\square

3.2.  A priori estimate

We now turn to the globalization problem for (
1.1
), which amounts to prove global existence for the solution
v
to (
3.7
) for general noise data
\mathbf{z}=(\mathbf{z}^{(1)},\mathbf{z}^{(2)},\mathbf{z}^{(3)})\in C(\mathbb{R},\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon})\times L^{q}_{\text{loc}}(\mathbb{R};\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon})^{2}
and initial condition
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
where
\varepsilon
and
q
are as in Proposition
3.1
to ensure local well-posedness. This in particular includes the case of the fully renormalized equation (
1.1
) but the more general framework allows to play with a range of noise data and initial data, see subsection
3.4
. In view of the blow-up criterion provided by Proposition
3.1
, it is enough to prove that the
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
norm of
v
stays bounded uniformly in time. Using the embedding

L^{3p-2}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}

for
p>\frac{4}{3\varepsilon}
, it comes down to a bound the
L^{3p-2}
norm of
v
for
p
large enough, this is the purpose of the rest of this section. Let
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
and consider
T\in(0,+\infty]
be the possible infinite blow up time in
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
of the solution.

We follow the argument from the usual
\Phi_{d}^{4}
equation with a coming down from infinity estimate. Due to the singularity of the environment, we have to work with paracontrolled expansion for the solution hence we are closed to the
\Phi_{3}^{4}
equation with Mourrat and Weber
[
17
]
than
\Phi_{2}^{4}
with Tsatsoulis and Weber
[
24
]
. Indeed, the idea in the usual
\Phi_{2}^{4}
is to test the equation against
v^{3p-2}
to obtain a priori bounds on the
L^{3p-2}
norm which is not possible at the level of
u
due to the irregularity of the linear term
. When
\mathcal{H}
is replaced by the Laplacian, one can use the integration by part

(3.13)

\langle\Delta v,v^{3p-2}\rangle=-\big\langle|\nabla u|^{2},u^{3p-3}\big\rangle

which is not possible for the Anderson Hamiltonian. To overcome this issue, we use that
\mathcal{H}
is a perturbation of the Laplacian which requires to work at the level of paracontrolled remainder
w=\Gamma^{-1}v
implicitly defined by

(3.14)

v=v\mathbin{\lx@scalerel@obj{\hbox to8.9pt{\vbox to8.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.45pt\lower-4.45pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.88 0 C 5.88 3.25 3.25 5.88 0 5.88 C -3.25 5.88 -5.88 3.25 -5.88 0 C -5.88 -3.25 -3.25 -5.88 0 -5.88 C 3.25 -5.88 5.88 -3.25 5.88 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.125pt}{-1.75pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -4.32 -2.42)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}X+w

for
v
in the Sobolev spaces associated to
\mathcal{H}
where
X=\Delta^{-1}\xi\in\mathcal{C}^{1-\kappa}
. In the following, we test the equation satified by
w
against
w^{3p-2}
a obtain a priori estimates. Using that
\Gamma
is continuous from
L^{p}
to itself, this gives the needed bound on
v
and will give global well-posedness. In fact, it will be important to introduce a truncation parameter
n>0
and rather consider the ansatz

v=v\mathbin{\lx@scalerel@obj{\hbox to8.9pt{\vbox to8.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.45pt\lower-4.45pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.88 0 C 5.88 3.25 3.25 5.88 0 5.88 C -3.25 5.88 -5.88 3.25 -5.88 0 C -5.88 -3.25 -3.25 -5.88 0 -5.88 C 3.25 -5.88 5.88 -3.25 5.88 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.125pt}{-1.75pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -4.32 -2.42)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}X_{>n}+w^{(n)}

where
X_{>n}=\Delta_{>n}X
. Such truncation is already important in recent construction of the Anderson Hamiltonian, see
[
3
,
15
,
19
]
and Appendix

A
for the full story. With this truncation, we are equiped with the one-to-one mapping
\Gamma_{n}:w^{(n)}\mapsto v
defined as the inverse mapping of

(3.15)

\Phi_{n}(v)=v-v\mathbin{\lx@scalerel@obj{\hbox to8.9pt{\vbox to8.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.45pt\lower-4.45pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.88 0 C 5.88 3.25 3.25 5.88 0 5.88 C -3.25 5.88 -5.88 3.25 -5.88 0 C -5.88 -3.25 -3.25 -5.88 0 -5.88 C 3.25 -5.88 5.88 -3.25 5.88 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.125pt}{-1.75pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -4.32 -2.42)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}X_{>n}

which is an invertible perturbation of the identity for
n=n(X)>0
large enough. An important property is that
w^{(n)}
inherits the same regularity properties as
w
, uniformly in
n
. The construction of
\mathcal{H}
also relies heavily on the fact that one can tune the paraproduct
\mathbin{\lx@scalerel@obj{\hbox to8.9pt{\vbox to8.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.45pt\lower-4.45pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.88 0 C 5.88 3.25 3.25 5.88 0 5.88 C -3.25 5.88 -5.88 3.25 -5.88 0 C -5.88 -3.25 -3.25 -5.88 0 -5.88 C 3.25 -5.88 5.88 -3.25 5.88 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.125pt}{-1.75pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -4.32 -2.42)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}
so that
v
is controlled by
w
in appropriate norms while the converse being always true, see Lemma
A.4
. We claim that it is still the case for
w^{(n)}
and that the bound holds uniformly in
n
. In particular, we will drop the parameter
n
in the notation
w^{(n)}
in the following to lighten the computations, it will be fix large enough depending only on
X
and universal constants.

Lemma 3.3 –

For any
n\geq 1
and
p>1
, there is a constant
C>0
that does not depend on
n
such that

C^{-1}\|v\|_{L^{p}}\leq\|w^{(n)}\|_{L^{p}}\leq C\|v\|_{L^{p}}

with
v=\Gamma_{n}w
.

Proof :

This follows from the continuity of
\Gamma_{n}
and
\Phi_{n}
. The constant is uniform in
n
since both applications converge to the identity as
n
goes to infinity. Thus this is a direct consequence of Lemma
A.4
and the fact that
\|X_{>n}\|_{B^{1-\kappa}_{q,\infty}}
is bounded by
\|X\|_{B^{1-\kappa}_{q,\infty}}
uniformly in
n
, for any
\kappa>0
and
q\geq 1
.

\square

This truncation will be important to make cubic terms involving
X_{>n}
small for
n
large enough. The construction of
\mathcal{H}
with this paracontrolled expression gives a remainder operator such that

\Gamma_{n}^{-1}\mathcal{H}\Gamma_{n}w=-\Delta w+\xi\mathbin{\lx@scalerel@obj{\hbox to9.14pt{\vbox to9.14pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.57158pt\lower-4.57158pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 6.05 0 C 6.05 3.34 3.34 6.05 0 6.05 C -3.34 6.05 -6.05 3.34 -6.05 0 C -6.05 -3.34 -3.34 -6.05 0 -6.05 C 3.34 -6.05 6.05 -3.34 6.05 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.125pt}{-1.74626pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -4.32 -2.42)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}w+R_{n}(w)

and
R_{n}:B^{\kappa}_{p,\infty}\to B^{-\kappa^{\prime}}_{p,\infty}
is continuous. While the norm of

We will keep

in our notations to make things easier to read, but one should really keep in mind that

. It is crucial noting that the leading power

comes with a positive sign. Note that while

, it will be controled using the parameter

. Testing equation (

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3p-2}\big(\|w_{t}\|_{L^{3p-2}}^{3p-2}-\|w_{0}\|_{L^{3p-2}}^{3p-2}\big)+(3p-3)\int_{0}^{t}\||\nabla w_{s}|^{2}w_{s}^{3p-4}\|_{L^{1}}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\|w_{s}\|_{L^{3p}}^{3p}\mathrm{d}s

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.125pt}{-1.74626pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -4.32 -2.42)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}w_{s}+w_{s}^{3}+Q(v_{s},w_{s},\mathbf{z}_{s})+R_{n}(w_{s})\big)\mathrm{d}s

and the fact that
e^{(t-s)\Delta}
is a regularizing operator, at the price of a diverging power of
t-s
. From there we will be able to close the estimate and obtain a
coming down from infinity
estimate. We now prove estimates on all the needed term to get a first a priori estimate with Proposition
3.9
. Remember that
p
is an even integer supposed to be large enough and
T\in(0,\infty]
is the maximal time of existence provided by the local existence Theorem
3.1
.

Lemma 3.4 –

For any
\kappa>0
and
0<s<t<T
, we have

\left|\int_{s}^{t}\langle\xi\mathbin{\lx@scalerel@obj{\hbox to9.14pt{\vbox to9.14pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.57158pt\lower-4.57158pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 6.05 0 C 6.05 3.34 3.34 6.05 0 6.05 C -3.34 6.05 -6.05 3.34 -6.05 0 C -6.05 -3.34 -3.34 -6.05 0 -6.05 C 3.34 -6.05 6.05 -3.34 6.05 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.125pt}{-1.74626pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -4.32 -2.42)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}w_{r},w_{r}^{3p-3}\rangle\mathrm{d}r\right|\lesssim\left(\int_{s}^{t}\|w_{r}\|_{L^{3p}}^{3p}\mathrm{d}r\right)^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\left(\int_{s}^{t}\|w_{r}\|^{p}_{B^{1+2\kappa}_{p,\infty}}\mathrm{d}r\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

where the implicit constant depends only on
p,\kappa
and
\xi
.

Proof :

Since the space white noise
\xi
belongs to
\mathcal{C}^{-1-\kappa}
for any
\kappa>0
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{t}\langle\xi\mathbin{\lx@scalerel@obj{\hbox to9.14pt{\vbox to9.14pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.57158pt\lower-4.57158pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 6.05 0 C 6.05 3.34 3.34 6.05 0 6.05 C -3.34 6.05 -6.05 3.34 -6.05 0 C -6.05 -3.34 -3.34 -6.05 0 -6.05 C 3.34 -6.05 6.05 -3.34 6.05 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.125pt}{-1.74626pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -4.32 -2.42)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}w_{s},w_{s}^{3p-3}\rangle\mathrm{d}s\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|w_{s}^{3p-3}\|_{L^{\frac{3p}{3p-3}}}\|\xi\mathbin{\lx@scalerel@obj{\hbox to9.14pt{\vbox to9.14pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.57158pt\lower-4.57158pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 6.05 0 C 6.05 3.34 3.34 6.05 0 6.05 C -3.34 6.05 -6.05 3.34 -6.05 0 C -6.05 -3.34 -3.34 -6.05 0 -6.05 C 3.34 -6.05 6.05 -3.34 6.05 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}w_{s}\|_{L^{p}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|w_{s}\|_{L^{3p}}^{3p-3}\|w_{s}\|_{B^{1+2\kappa}_{p,\infty}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int_{0}^{t}\|w_{s}\|_{L^{3p}}^{3p}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\|w_{s}\|^{p}_{B^{1+2\kappa}_{p,\infty}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

using paraproduct and resonant term estimates as well as Hölder inequality and the relation

1=\frac{p-1}{p}+\frac{1}{p}=\frac{3p-3}{3p}+\frac{1}{p}

for
p\geq 1
which will be used multiple times in the following proofs.

\square

Lemma 3.5 –

For any
0<s<t<T
, we have

\left|\int_{s}^{t}\langle R_{n}(w_{r}),w_{r}^{3p-3}\rangle\mathrm{d}r\right|\lesssim_{n}\left(\int_{s}^{t}\|w_{r}\|_{L^{3p}}^{3p}\mathrm{d}r\right)^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\left(\int_{s}^{t}\|w_{r}\|^{p}_{B^{\varepsilon}_{p,\infty}}\mathrm{d}r\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

Proof :

Lemma
A.5
gives that the remainder term is a bounded operator

R_{n}:B_{q,\infty}^{\kappa}\to B_{q,\infty}^{-\kappa^{\prime}}

for arbitrary
\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}>0
and
q\in[1,\infty]
. This yields

\displaystyle\big|\langle R_{n}(w),w^{3p-3}\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|R_{n}(w)\|_{B^{-\kappa}_{\frac{3p}{2},1}}\|w^{3p-3}\|_{B^{\kappa}_{\frac{3p}{3p-2},\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|R_{n}(w)\|_{B^{-\kappa}_{\frac{3p}{2},\infty}}\|w^{3p-3}\|_{B^{\kappa}_{\frac{3p}{3p-2},\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n}\|w\|_{B^{\kappa}_{\frac{3p}{2},\infty}}\|w^{3p-3}\|_{B^{\kappa}_{\frac{3p}{3p-2},\infty}}

and we have

\displaystyle\|w^{3p-3}\|_{B^{\kappa}_{\frac{3p}{3p-2},\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|w^{3p-4}\|_{L^{\frac{3p}{3p-4}}}\|w\|_{B^{\kappa}_{\frac{3p}{2},\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|w\|_{L^{3p}}^{3p-4}\|w\|_{B^{\kappa}_{\frac{3p}{2},\infty}}

using the nonlinear estimate from Lemma
B.1
.
We interpolate
B^{\kappa}_{\frac{3p}{2},\infty}
between
L^{\frac{3p}{2}}
and
B^{2\kappa}_{\frac{3p}{2},\infty}
using Lemma
B.2
.

\displaystyle\|w\|_{B^{\kappa}_{\frac{3p}{2},\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|w\|_{B^{2\kappa}_{\frac{3p}{2},\infty}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|w\|_{L^{\frac{3p}{2}}}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

The estimate follows by Hölder inequality with

\displaystyle\big|\int_{s}^{t}\langle R_{n}(w_{r}),w_{r}^{3p-3}\rangle\mathrm{d}r\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n}\int_{s}^{t}\|w_{r}\|_{L^{3p}}^{3p-3}\|w_{r}\|_{B^{1+\varepsilon}_{p,\infty}}\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n}\left(\int_{s}^{t}\|w_{r}\|^{3p}_{L^{3p}}\mathrm{d}r\right)^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\left(\int_{s}^{t}\|w_{r}\|^{p}_{B^{2\kappa}_{p,\infty}}\mathrm{d}r\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

and setting
2\kappa=\varepsilon
.

\square

for any
\kappa\in(0,1)
using Hölder inequality. Similar computations give

\displaystyle\left|\int_{s}^{t}\langle Q_{1}(v_{r},w_{r}),w_{r}^{3p-3}\rangle\mathrm{d}r\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|X_{>n}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\kappa}}\int_{s}^{t}\|v_{r}\|_{L^{3p}}^{3}\|w_{r}\|_{L^{3p}}^{3p-3}\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\|X_{>n}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\kappa}}^{k}\int_{s}^{t}\|v_{r}\|_{L^{3p}}^{k}\|w_{r}\|_{L^{3p}}^{3p-k}\mathrm{d}r

where the implicit constant does not depend on
n
. Recall from Lemma
A.4
that
v
is controlled by
w
uniformly in
n
. Thus, choosing
n
large enough depending on
\delta,p
and
X
, we get

\left|\int_{s}^{t}\langle Q_{1}(v_{r},w_{r}),w_{r}^{3p-3}\rangle\mathrm{d}r\right|\leq\delta\int_{s}^{t}\|w_{r}\|_{L^{3p}}^{3p}\mathrm{d}r

which completes the proof.

\square

For the other nonlinear terms, note that only the regularizing properties of
\cdot\mathbin{\lx@scalerel@obj{\hbox to8.9pt{\vbox to8.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.45pt\lower-4.45pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.88 0 C 5.88 3.25 3.25 5.88 0 5.88 C -3.25 5.88 -5.88 3.25 -5.88 0 C -5.88 -3.25 -3.25 -5.88 0 -5.88 C 3.25 -5.88 5.88 -3.25 5.88 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.125pt}{-1.75pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -4.32 -2.42)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}X_{>n}
are used. As such, the estimates all end up being uniform in the truncation parameter
n
.

Lemma 3.7 –

For any
\delta>0
, there exists a constant
c>0
that depends only on
\delta,p,\varepsilon
such that for any
0<s<t<T
, we have

\left|\int_{s}^{t}\langle Q_{2}(v_{r},\mathbf{z}_{r}),w_{r}^{3p-3}\rangle\mathrm{d}r\right|\leq\delta\int_{s}^{t}\|w_{r}\|_{L^{3p}}^{3p}\mathrm{d}r+cK_{t}\big(1+\int_{s}^{t}\|w_{r}\|_{B_{p,\infty}^{2\varepsilon}}^{p}\mathrm{d}r\big)

where the constant
K_{t}
is defined by

(3.18)

\displaystyle{}K_{t}=\sup_{0\leq r\leq t}\|\mathbf{z}_{r}^{(1)}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}^{2p}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathbf{z}^{(2)}_{r}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}^{3p}\mathrm{d}r+\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathbf{z}^{(3)}_{r}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}^{3p}\mathrm{d}r.

Proof :

The major issue with

(3.19)

Q_{2}(v,\mathbf{z})=v^{2}\mathbf{z}^{(1)}+v\mathbf{z}^{(2)}+\mathbf{z}^{(3)}

is handling the quadratic part
v^{2}\mathbf{z}^{(1)}
, to that end we further expand

as

\displaystyle=(v\mathbin{\lx@scalerel@obj{\hbox to8.9pt{\vbox to8.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.45pt\lower-4.45pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.88 0 C 5.88 3.25 3.25 5.88 0 5.88 C -3.25 5.88 -5.88 3.25 -5.88 0 C -5.88 -3.25 -3.25 -5.88 0 -5.88 C 3.25 -5.88 5.88 -3.25 5.88 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

since
