\displaystyle=\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{t,t+h}

hence the expression

(4.6)

v_{t,t+h}=e^{-h\mathcal{H}}u(t\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})-\int_{0}^{h}e^{-(h-r)\mathcal{H}}\sum_{j=0}^{3}v_{t+r}^{j}\mathbf{z}_{t+r}^{(3-j)}\mathrm{d}r.

From the binomial expansion Lemma
2.2
and the relation between
v_{t}
and
v_{t,t+h}
, we get

\sum_{j=0}^{3}v_{t+r}^{j}\mathbf{z}_{t+r}^{(3-j)}=\sum_{j=0}^{3}v_{t,t+r}^{j}\mathbf{z}_{t,t+r}^{(3-j)}

and the expected expression of
v_{t,t+h}
follows.

\square

Corollary 4.2 –

Let
u(\cdot\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})=\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}+v(\cdot\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})
be the solution to equation (
1.1
). Then for any
\varepsilon>0
small enough and
p
large enough, we have

\sup_{u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}\sup_{t\geq 0}\big(1\wedge t^{\frac{3p-2}{2}}\big)\mathbb{E}\left[\|u(t\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}^{3p-2}\right]<+\infty.

Proof :

First note that

\sup_{u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}\sup_{0\leq t\leq 1}\big(1\wedge t^{\frac{3p-2}{2}}\big)\mathbb{E}\left[\|u(t\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}^{3p-2}\right]<+\infty

is a direct consequence of Proposition
3.14
. The bound when the supremum ranges over all positive times does not follow directly however as we cannot bound the expectation of
\sup_{t\geq 0}\tilde{K}_{t}
. We are able to circumvent this issue using that the noise data
\mathbf{z}
has the same law on intervals of the same size and the bound in Proposition
3.14
does not depend on the initial condition. Let
t\geq 1
and observe that for
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
, we have

(4.7)

u(t\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})=\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{t-1,t}+v_{t-1,t}

where
v_{t-1,t}
is given by Lemma
4.1
. In particular,
v_{t-1,t-1+\cdot}
solves the equation (
3.7
) with noise
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{z}_{t-1,t-1+\cdot}
and initial data
u(t-1\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})
. Proposition
3.14
with the continuity of
\Gamma^{-1}
gives

(4.8)

\|v_{t-1,t}\|_{L^{3p-2}}\lesssim 1\wedge c\tilde{K}_{t-1,t}

with

\tilde{K}_{t-1,t}=\big(\sup_{0\leq r\leq 1}\|\mathbf{z}_{t-1,t-1+r}^{(1)}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}+\|\mathbf{z}_{t-1,t-1+r}^{(2)}\|_{L_{r}^{{q}}\big([0,1],\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}\big)}+\|\mathbf{z}_{t-1,t-1+r}^{(3)}\|_{L_{r}^{{q}}\big([0,1],\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}\big)}\big)^{b}.

From Lemma
2.3
, the law of
\mathbf{z}_{t-1,t-1+\cdot}
does not depend on
t
, one can take the expectation and get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|u(t\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}^{3p-2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{t-1,t}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}^{3p-2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\|\tilde{v}_{t-1,t}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}^{3p-2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{t-1,t}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}^{3p-2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\|\tilde{v}_{t-1,t}\|_{L^{3p-2}}^{3p-2}\right]

where the right-hand side is bounded uniformly both in
t
and
u_{0}
. This gives

\sup_{u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{\varepsilon}}\sup_{t\leq r\leq t+1}\big(1\wedge r^{\frac{3p-2}{2}}\big)\mathbb{E}\left[\|u(r\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}^{3p-2}\right]<+\infty

which completes the proof since
t\geq 1
is arbitrary.

\square

4.1.  The transition semi-group and Feller property

As we intend to prove Markov property for
t\mapsto u(t\hskip 1.42271pt;\cdot)
, we give a more precise description of
\tilde{v}
defined in Lemma
4.1
. For
h\geq 0
, we write
v(h\hskip 1.42271pt;v_{0}\hskip 1.42271pt;\mathbf{y})
the solution to (
3.7
) at time
h
, starting from
v_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
and driven by noise data
\mathbf{y}
. This notation will simplify the proof of the following proposition. It proves that
t\mapsto P_{t}
is indeed a semigroup which gives the Markov property as direct consequence.

Proposition 4.3 –

Let
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
and
t\geq 0
. Then for any
\varphi\in C_{b}\big(\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}\big)
, we have

\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi\big(u(t+h\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})\big)\big|\mathcal{F}_{t}\Big]=P_{h}\varphi\big(u(t\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})\big)

for
h\geq 0
.

Proof :

In view of Lemma
4.1
, we have

u(t+h\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})=\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{t,t+h}+v\big(h;u(t\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})\hskip 1.42271pt;\mathbf{z}_{t,t+\cdot}\big)

where
u(t\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})
is
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-measurable while
\mathbf{z}_{t,t+\cdot}
is
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-independent, see Lemma
2.3
. Thus, according to Proposition 1.12 in
[
10
]
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\varphi\big(u(t+h\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})\big)\middle|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\varphi\big(\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{t,t+h}+v\big(h\hskip 1.42271pt;u(t\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})\hskip 1.42271pt;\mathbf{z}_{t,t+\cdot}\big)\big)\middle|\mathcal{F}_{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\varphi\big(\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{t,t+h}+v\big(h\hskip 1.42271pt;u(t\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})\hskip 1.42271pt;\mathbf{z}_{t,t+\cdot}\big)\big)\right]

where the expectation is with respect to the data
\mathbf{z}_{t,t+\cdot}
. Moreover, since
\mathbf{z}_{t,t+\cdot}
and
\mathbf{z}
have the same law with again Lemma
2.3
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\varphi\big(\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{t,t+h}+v\big(h\hskip 1.42271pt;v_{0}\hskip 1.42271pt;\mathbf{z}_{t,t+\cdot}\big)\big)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\varphi\big(\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{0,h}+v\big(h\hskip 1.42271pt;v_{0}\hskip 1.42271pt;\mathbf{z}\big)\big)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\varphi\big(u(h\hskip 1.42271pt;v_{0})\big)\right]

\displaystyle=P_{h}\varphi\big(v_{0}\big)

for any
v_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
. Applying this in
v_{0}=u(t\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})
completes the proof.

\square

The Feller property for the semigroup
(P_{t})_{t\geq 0}
follows as a direct consequence of Proposition
3.1
. Indeed, not only do we obtain local well-posedness, but also that the solution
v
to (
3.7
) depends continuously on the initial data in
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
. In the strong Feller property, one only assumes the functionnal to be a Borelian bounded function on
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
, this will be proved in the rest of the section.

Corollary 4.4 –

The semigroup
(P_{t})_{t\geq 0}
has the Feller property, that is

(4.9)

\forall\varphi\in C_{b}\big(\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}\big),P_{t}\varphi\in C_{b}\big(\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}\big)

for any
t\geq 0
.

We conclude this subsection with the existence of invariant measures for the semigroup
(P_{t})_{t\geq 0}
. We prove an ergodic theorem for the dual semigroup
(P^{*}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
acting on the set of probability measures on
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
. The proof relies on a compactness argument hence uniqueness of an invariant measure is not ensured at this stage.

Proposition 4.5 –

Let
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
. There is a increasing sequence of diverging times
(t_{k})_{k\geq 0}
and a probability measure
\mu_{u_{0}}
on
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
such that

\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{1}{t_{k}}\int_{0}^{t_{k}}P_{r}^{*}\delta_{u_{0}}\mathrm{d}r=\mu_{u_{0}}

where the convergence holds weakly. As such,
\mu_{u_{0}}
is an invariant measure for
(P_{t})_{t\geq 0}
acting on
C_{b}\big(\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}\big)
.

Proof :

We intend to use Krylov-Bogoliubov theorem as given in theorem 3.1.1 of
[
8
]
. Set

R_{t}:=\frac{1}{t}\int_{0}^{t}P_{r}^{*}\delta_{u_{0}}\mathrm{d}r

for
t>0
, it is enough to prove that the sequence
(R_{t_{k}})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is uniformly tight as a sequence of probability measures on
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
for an increasing sequence of diverging times
(t_{k})_{k\geq 0}
. In particular, we are not bothered by the behavior of
R_{t}
close to
0
since only when large times matter. Let
p>1
be as in Proposition
4.2
,
t\geq 0
and
R>0
. We begin with the following observation that arises from the definition of
P^{*}
. Using Markov and Jensen’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle R_{t}\left(\{u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon_{0}}\,,\ \|u_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon_{0}}}>R\}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t}\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{P}\left(\|u(r\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon_{0}}}>R\right)\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{Rt}\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\|u(r\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon_{0}}}^{3p-2}\right]^{\frac{1}{3p-2}}\mathrm{d}r.

Then Proposition
4.2
yields

\displaystyle R_{t}\left(\{u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon_{0}}\,,\ \|u_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon_{0}}}>R\}\right)\lesssim\frac{1+t}{Rt}.

Given any sequence of increasing diverging times
(t_{k})_{k\geq 0}
, we have

(4.10)

\sup_{k\geq 0}\frac{1+t_{k}}{Rt_{k}}<\infty

while the set
\{u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon_{0}}\,,\ \|u_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon_{0}}}\leq R\}
is a compact subset of
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
for any
\varepsilon>\varepsilon_{0}
hence
(R_{t_{k}})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is tight. As such, there exists a limiting measure
\mu_{u_{0}}
on
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
such that
R_{t_{k}}
converges weakly to
\mu_{u_{0}}
up to extraction. Theorem 3.1.1 in
[
8
]
then ensures that
\mu_{u_{0}}
is an invariant measure to the semigroup
(P_{t})_{t\geq 0}
acting on
C_{b}\big(\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}\big)
.

\square

4.2.  Investigating the strong Feller property

As far as the strong Feller property is concerned, the idea is to obtain a Lipschitz bound on the semigroup
P_{t}
which will ensure that continuity is recovered even for merely borelian bounded test functions
\varphi
. This leads to investigate the behavior of
u
as a function of its initial condition
u_{0}
. For the computations to make sense, it is necessary to work at the level of the truncated equation so that the Wick power is an actual polynomial rather than a renormalized object. Thus, let
P_{t}^{N}
be the transition semigroup associated to equation (
3.1
)

(4.11)

\partial_{t}u_{N}+\mathcal{H}u_{N}+\mathbf{:}u_{N}^{3}\mathbf{:}=\sqrt{2}\Pi_{N}\zeta

defined as

(4.12)

P_{t}^{N}(\varphi)(u_{0})=\mathbb{E}\big[\varphi(u_{N}(t\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})\big]

for
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
. The main result of this section is the following local Lipschitz bound on the differential of the truncated semigroup which gives the strong Feller property with Corollary
4.12
.

Proposition 4.6 –

Let
\varepsilon,\sigma,\delta>0
and
p\geq 1
such that
4.7
,
3.16
and
4.8
hold true. Assume also that
\delta+p\sigma+(p-1)\varepsilon+(\sigma-\varepsilon)<1
. Then there exists some constant
\gamma>0
such that for any
N\geq 1
,
\varphi\in C_{b}\left(\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}\right)
,
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
and
h\in L^{2}\cap\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}
, we have

\left|\mathrm{d}(P_{t}^{N}\varphi)(u_{0})\cdot h\right|\lesssim\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|h\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}}\big(1+t^{-\frac{1+(p+1)\sigma+p\varepsilon+\delta}{2}}\big)(1+\|u_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}})^{\gamma}

for any
t>0
.

We first investigate the behavior of
u
under variation of the initial condition. Recall equation
3.1
, if we set

(4.13)

\displaystyle{}\eta^{h}_{N}=\mathrm{d}(u_{N})(u_{0})\cdot h

for
u_{0},h\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
, then
\eta^{h}_{N}
solves the linear equation

(4.14)

\displaystyle{}\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\eta^{h}_{N}+\mathcal{H}\eta^{h}_{N}+3\mathbf{:}u_{N}^{2}\mathbf{:}\,\eta^{h}_{N}=0\\
{\eta^{h}_{N}}_{|t=0}=h.\end{cases}

The following lemma is what calls for the use of an alternative semi group in order to kill the blow-up obtained using Grönwall’s estimate following
[
7
]
.

Lemma 4.7 –

Let
\sigma>\varepsilon
,
\delta>0
small enough and
p\geq 1
large enough satisfying
2\sigma+\varepsilon+\delta+1<\frac{2(p-1)}{p}
. Then there exists a constant
c>0
, uniform in
t\in(0,1]
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
and
h\in\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}
such that

\|\eta^{h}_{N}(t)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}}\lesssim t^{-(\sigma+\delta)/2}\|h\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}}e^{c\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathbf{:}u_{N}^{2}(s)\mathbf{:}\|_{H^{-\varepsilon}}^{p}\mathrm{d}s}.

Note that
\eta^{h}_{N}
has positive spatial regularity as soon as
t>0
, this is due to the regularizing effects of
e^{-t\mathcal{H}}
with Schauder estimates, hence the diverging factor as
t
goes to
0
. Also, a more direct bound would yield a norm
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
in the exponential as well as a lighter condition on the exponents however, as we want to use a Bismuth-Elworthy-Li formula in the following, it is more convenient to work with a Hilbert space norm of
u_{N}
for differentiation matters.

Proof :

Write the mild formulation of equation (
4.14
) and take the
\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}
norm, this yields

\displaystyle\|\eta^{h}_{N}(t)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|e^{-t\mathcal{H}}h\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}}+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|e^{-(t-s)\mathcal{H}}\big(\mathbf{:}u_{N}^{2}(s)\mathbf{:}\eta^{h}_{N}(s)\big)\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{-(\sigma+\delta)/2}\|h\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}}+\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-(\sigma+\varepsilon)/2}\left\|e^{-\frac{t-s}{2}\mathcal{H}}\big(\mathbf{:}u_{N}^{2}(s)\mathbf{:}\eta^{h}_{N}(s)\big)\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{-(\sigma+\delta)/2}\|h\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}}+\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-(\sigma+\varepsilon)/2}\left\|e^{-\frac{t-s}{2}\mathcal{H}}\big(\mathbf{:}u_{N}^{2}(s)\mathbf{:}\eta^{h}_{N}(s)\big)\right\|_{H^{1-\varepsilon}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{-(\sigma+\delta)/2}\|h\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}}+\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-(\sigma+\varepsilon+1)/2}\left\|\mathbf{:}u_{N}^{2}(s)\mathbf{:}\right\|_{H^{-\varepsilon}}\left\|\eta^{h}_{N}(s)\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}}\mathrm{d}s

using Schauder estimates and Besov embeddings. Setting
\lambda(t):=t^{(\sigma+\delta)/2}\|\eta^{h}_{N}(t)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}}
, Hölder inequality yields

\displaystyle\lambda(t)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|h\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}}+\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-(\sigma+\varepsilon+1)/2}s^{-(\sigma+\delta)/2}\left\|\mathbf{:}u_{N}^{2}(s)\mathbf{:}\right\|_{H^{-\varepsilon}}\lambda(s)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\|h\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}}+\left(\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{p(\sigma+\varepsilon+1)}{2(p-1)}}s^{-\frac{p(\sigma+\delta)}{2(p-1)}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\mathbf{:}u_{N}^{2}(s)\mathbf{:}\right\|_{H^{-\varepsilon}}^{p}\lambda(s)^{p}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

for any
p\geq 1
. Choosing
p
such that
2\sigma+\varepsilon+\delta+1<\frac{2(p-1)}{p}
, the first integral is bounded uniformly in
t\in(0,1]
so that Grönwall inequality on
\lambda(t)^{p}
yields the bound

\|\eta^{h}_{N}(t)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}}\lesssim t^{-(\sigma+\delta)/2}\|h\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}}e^{c\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathbf{:}u_{N}^{2}(s)\mathbf{:}\|_{H^{-\varepsilon}}^{p}\mathrm{d}s}.

\square

4.3.  Estimates on the auxiliary semigroup

Because of the exponential on the right-hand side of Lemma
4.7
, we will rather consider the Feynman-Kac semigroup
S^{N}_{t}
defined by

(4.15)

\displaystyle{}S_{t}^{N}\varphi(u_{0}):=\mathbb{E}\Big[e^{-\int_{0}^{t}V\left(u_{N}(s\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})\right)\mathrm{d}s}\varphi\big(u_{N}(s\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})\big)\Big]

for a well-chosen non-negative functional
V
of the solution
u_{N}
. In particular, the Kolmogorov equation for
S_{t}^{N}
is the same as for
P_{t}^{N}
with an additional shift by the potential. At the level of the mild formulation, this reads

(4.16)

\displaystyle{}P_{t}^{N}\varphi=S_{t}^{N}\varphi+\int_{0}^{t}S_{t-s}^{N}\big(VP_{s}^{N}\varphi\big)\mathrm{d}s.

Thus, while we hope to obtain better estimats on
S_{t}^{N}
thanks to the exponential factor, this will be sufficient for establishing similar bounds on
P_{t}^{N}
. The method is taken from the recent work
[
7
]
by Da Prato and Debussche on the usual
\Phi_{2}^{4}
equation on the torus. We refer to Da Prato and Zabczyk’s book
[
9
]
and references therein for more details on the Feynman Kac semigroups. In view of Lemma
4.7
, set

V(u_{N}):=\tilde{c}\|\mathbf{:}u_{N}^{2}\mathbf{:}\|_{H^{-\varepsilon}}^{p}

for a positive constant
\tilde{c}
to be chosen later, thus following moments bound on
V
holds. This is where it better to work in an Hilbert space since we will differentiate
V
.

Corollary 4.8 –

Let
N\geq 1
and
\sigma>\varepsilon>0
be small enough so that
3.15
,
3.16
,
3.18
. For
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
and
p\geq 1
, there exist a constant
\gamma>0
depending on
\varepsilon
and
p
such that

\mathbb{E}\Big[\|\mathbf{:}u_{N}(t)^{2}\mathbf{:}\|_{H^{-\varepsilon}}^{p}\Big]\lesssim t^{-\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}p}(1+\|u_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}})^{\gamma}

uniformly in
t\in(0,1)
and
N\geq 1
.

\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(t))^{2}\mathbf{:}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Pi_{N}\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(t)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{:}(\Pi_{N}\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(t))^{2}\mathbf{:}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}+\|\tilde{v}_{N}(t)\|_{H^{\sigma}}^{2}+\|v_{N}(t)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+t^{-\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}}\|u_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}\big(\|v_{N}(t)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}+\|\Pi_{N}\hbox to3.38pt{\vbox to8.09pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.59164pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.8pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0.14 8.3 M 1.93 8.3 C 1.93 9.29 1.13 10.09 0.14 10.09 C -0.85 10.09 -1.65 9.29 -1.65 8.3 C -1.65 7.32 -0.85 6.51 0.14 6.51 C 1.13 6.51 1.93 7.32 1.93 8.3 Z M 0.14 8.3}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}(t)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}\big).

Taking the
p
-th moment, we can conclude using Corollary
3.16
for the Hölder norm and Proposition
3.18
for the Sobolev norm.

\square

Let us recall the following Bismuth-Elworthy-Li formula from
[
9
]
.

Proposition 4.9 –

For any
\varphi\in C_{b}\left(\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}\right)
, the semigroup
S_{T}^{N}\varphi
is differentiable in any direction
h\in L^{2}
and we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}(S_{t}^{N}\varphi)(u_{0})\cdot h=\mathbb{E}\Big[

\displaystyle e^{-\int_{0}^{t}V(u_{N}(s\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0}))\mathrm{d}s}\varphi\big(u_{N}(t\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})\big)\times

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{1}{t}\int_{0}^{t}\eta_{N}^{h}(s)\zeta(\mathrm{d}s)+\int_{0}^{t}(1-\frac{s}{t})\mathrm{d}V(u_{N}(s\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0}))\cdot\eta^{h}_{N}(s)\mathrm{d}s\Big)\Big].

This is precisely where working at the level of the approximated solution
u_{N}
rather than the limiting one
u
is needed. To the best of our knowledge, for the identity above to hold it is required that the Feynman Kac potential is a Lipschitz function of the solution. This is however not an issue as all the estimates are uniform in
N\geq 1
. Abusing notations, we denote by
\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}
the infimum of
\{M\geq 0\,,\ \forall u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}\,,\ |\varphi(u_{0})|\leq M\}
for
\varphi\in\mathcal{B}_{b}
the space of Borelian bounded functions on
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
.

Proposition 4.10 –

Let
\varepsilon,\sigma,\delta>0
and
p\geq 1
such that
4.7
,
3.16
and
4.8
hold true. Assume also that
\delta+p\sigma+(p-1)\varepsilon<2
, then there exists a constant
\gamma>0
such that for any
N\geq 1
,
\varphi\in C_{b}\left(\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}\right)
,
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
and
h\in L^{2}\cap\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}
, we have

\left|\mathrm{d}(S_{t}^{N}\varphi)(u_{0})\cdot h\right|\leq C\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|h\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}}\left(t^{-\frac{1+\sigma+\delta}{2}}+(1+\|u_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}})^{\gamma}\right).

Proof :

Let
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
and
h\in L^{2}\cap\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}
as well as
\sigma,\delta,p
be as in Lemma
4.7
. Using Proposition
4.9
, we bound

\mathbf{A}:=\mathbb{E}\Big[e^{-\int_{0}^{t}V(u_{N}(s\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0}))\mathrm{d}s}\varphi\big(u_{N}(t\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})\big)\frac{1}{t}\int_{0}^{t}\eta_{N}^{h}(s)\zeta(\mathrm{d}s)\Big]

and

\mathbf{B}:=\mathbb{E}\Big[e^{-\int_{0}^{t}V(u_{N}(s\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0}))\mathrm{d}s}\varphi\big(u_{N}(t;u_{0})\big)\int_{0}^{t}\big(1-\frac{s}{t}\big)\mathrm{d}V\big(u_{N}(s\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})\big)\cdot\eta_{N}^{h}(s)\mathrm{d}s\Big]

separately. For the first term, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{t}\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|e^{-\int_{0}^{t}V(u_{N}(s\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0}))\mathrm{d}s}\int_{0}^{t}\eta_{N}^{h}(s)\zeta(\mathrm{d}s)\Big|^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{t}\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2\int_{0}^{s}V(u_{N}(r\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0}))\mathrm{d}r}\big|\eta_{N}^{h}(s)\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and Itô formula. Choosing the constant
\tilde{c}
in the definition of
V
large enough, Lemma
4.7
yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}e^{-2\int_{0}^{s}V(u_{N}(r\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0}))\mathrm{d}r}\left|\eta_{N}^{h}(s)\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|h\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}}\left(\int_{0}^{t}s^{\sigma+\delta}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{\frac{1-\sigma-\delta}{2}}\|h\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}}

so that

\mathbf{A}\lesssim t^{-\frac{1+\sigma+\delta}{2}}\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|h\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}}.

For the second term, we have

\mathbf{B}\leq\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{-2\int_{0}^{t}V(u_{N}(s\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0}))\mathrm{d}s}\left|\int_{0}^{t}\big(1-\frac{s}{t}\big)\mathrm{d}V\big(u_{N}(s\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})\big)\eta_{N}^{h}(s)\mathrm{d}s\right|^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality once again. Recall that

V(u_{N}(s\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0}))=\tilde{c}\|\mathbf{:}u_{N}(s;u_{0})^{2}\mathbf{:}\|_{H^{-\varepsilon}}^{p}

so that we can easily differentiate
V
, that is

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}V\big(u_{N}(s\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})\big)\cdot\eta_{N}^{h}(s)=2p\|\mathbf{:}u_{N}(s\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})^{2}\mathbf{:}\|_{H^{-\varepsilon}}^{p-2}\left\langle\mathbf{:}u_{N}(s\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})^{2}\mathbf{:},u_{N}(s\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})\eta_{N}^{h}(s)\right\rangle_{H^{-\varepsilon}}

and therefore

\mathbf{B}\leq\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{-2\int_{0}^{t}V(u_{N}(s\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0}))\mathrm{d}s}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathbf{:}u_{N}(s\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})^{2}\mathbf{:}\|_{H^{-\varepsilon}}^{p-1}\|u_{N}(s\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})\|_{H^{-\varepsilon}}\|\eta_{N}^{h}(s)\|_{\mathcal{C}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Using once again Lemma
4.7
to handle the
\eta_{N}^{h}
term as well as Minkowski and Cauchy-Schwarz inequalities we get

\displaystyle\mathbf{B}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|h\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathbf{:}u_{N}(s\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})^{2}\mathbf{:}\|_{H^{-\varepsilon}}^{p-1}\|u_{N}(s\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})\|_{H^{-\varepsilon}}s^{-\frac{\sigma+\delta}{2}}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|h\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\Big[\|\mathbf{:}u_{N}(s\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})^{2}\mathbf{:}\|_{H^{-\varepsilon}}^{2p-2}\|u_{N}(s\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})\|^{2}_{H^{-\varepsilon}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}s^{-\frac{\sigma+\delta}{2}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|h\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\Big[\|\mathbf{:}u_{N}(s\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})^{2}\mathbf{:}\|_{H^{-\varepsilon}}^{4p-4}\Big]^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\|u_{N}(s\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})\|^{4}_{H^{-\varepsilon}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{4}}s^{-\frac{\sigma+\delta}{2}}\mathrm{d}s.

Using the moments bounds
3.16
and
4.8
, this yields

\mathbf{B}\lesssim\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|h\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}}(1+\|u_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}})^{\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}s^{-\frac{\sigma+\delta}{2}-(p-1)\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}}\mathrm{d}s

and provided
\delta+p\sigma+(p-1)\varepsilon<2
, the time integral is bounded uniformly in
[0,1]
and the estimate follows.

\square

Note that in view of the definition of
S_{t}^{N}
(
4.15
) and the bound
4.8
,
S_{t}^{N}
can be extended from bounded borelian functions
\varphi
on
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
to functions under the form
V\varphi
, hence the following counterpart to the previous estimate.

Proposition 4.11 –

Let
\varepsilon,\sigma,\delta>0
and
p\geq 1
such that
4.7
,
3.16
and
4.8
hold true. Assume also that
\delta+p\sigma+(p-1)\varepsilon<2
, then there exists some a
\gamma>0
such that for any
N\geq 1
,
\varphi\in C_{b}\left(\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}\right)
,
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
and
h\in L^{2}\cap\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}
, we have

\left|\mathrm{d}(S_{t}^{N}(V\varphi))(u_{0})\cdot h\right|\leq C\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|h\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}}t^{-\frac{1+(p+1)\sigma+p\varepsilon+\delta}{2}}(1+\|u_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}})^{\gamma}.

Proof :

The proof follows the exact same lines as the one above, up to the multiplicative term that appears when using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality
\mathbb{E}\left[V\big(u_{N}(t\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})\big)^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}
when estimating
\mathbf{A}
and
\mathbf{B}
. This is handled by Corollary
4.8
and the estimate follows from

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}+\mathbf{B}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|h\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}}\left(t^{-\frac{1+\sigma+\delta}{2}}+(1+\|u_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}})^{\gamma}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathbf{:}u_{N}(t\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})^{2}\mathbf{:}\|_{H^{-\varepsilon}}^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|h\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}}t^{-p\frac{\sigma+\varepsilon}{2}}\left(t^{-\frac{1+\sigma+\delta}{2}}+(1+\|u_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}})^{\gamma}\right)(1+\|u_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}})^{\gamma^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|h\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}}t^{-\frac{1+(p+1)\sigma+p\varepsilon+\delta}{2}}(1+\|u_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}})^{\gamma^{\prime\prime}}

for yet another exponent
\gamma^{\prime\prime}>0
.

\square

We can now exploit relation (
4.16
) in order to obtain similar estimates on the transition semigroup
P_{t}^{N}
and prove our main result.

Proof of Proposition
4.6
:

Note that the assumption on the different parameters indeed allows to use the previous proposition. Thus, differentiating relation (
4.16
) in the direction
h
and injecting the estimates of Propositions
4.10
and
4.11
, we get

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{d}(P_{t}^{N}\varphi)(u_{0})\cdot h\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\mathrm{d}(S_{t}^{N}\varphi)(u_{0})\cdot h\right|+\int_{0}^{t}\left|\mathrm{d}\big(S_{t-s}^{N}(VP_{s}^{N}\varphi)\big)(u_{0})\cdot h\right|\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|h\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}}\left(t^{-\frac{1+\sigma+\delta}{2}}+(1+\|u_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}})^{\gamma}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{0}^{t}\|P_{s}\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}(t-s)^{-\frac{1+(p+1)\sigma+p\varepsilon+\delta}{2}}\mathrm{d}s\|h\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}}(1+\|u_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}})^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|h\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}}\left(t^{-\frac{1+\sigma+\delta}{2}}+(1+\|u_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}})^{\gamma}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-\frac{1+(p+1)\sigma+p\varepsilon+\delta}{2}}\mathrm{d}s\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|h\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}}(1+\|u_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}})^{\gamma}

where in the last inequality we used

\|P_{t}\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}

which follows directly from the definition of the semigroup. The assumption on the indices ensures the time integral converges and is bounded uniformly in
t\in[0,1]
, hence the claim.

\square

Corollary 4.12 –

Let
\varepsilon,\sigma,\delta>0
and
p\geq 1
such that
4.6
holds true. Then there exists a constant
\gamma>0
such that for any bounded borelian function
\varphi
on
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
, we have

\left|(P_{t}\varphi)(u_{0})-(P_{t}\varphi)(\tilde{u}_{0})\right|\lesssim\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|u_{0}-\tilde{u}_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}}\big(1+t^{-\frac{1+(p+1)\sigma+p\varepsilon+\delta}{2}}\big)(1+\|u_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}}+\|\tilde{u}_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}})^{\gamma}

for any
t>0
, initial data
u_{0},\tilde{u}_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
and direction
h\in\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}
. In particular, the semigroup
(P_{t})_{t\geq 0}
has the strong Feller property, that is

(4.17)

\forall\varphi\in\mathcal{B}_{b}\big(\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}\big),P_{t}\varphi\in C_{b}\big(\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}\big)

for any
t>0
.

Proof :

First, since
L^{2}\cap\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}
is dense in
\mathcal{C}^{-\delta}
, Proposition
4.6
ensures that
h\mapsto\mathrm{d}(P_{t}^{N})(u_{0})\cdot h
extends uniquely as a bounded linear form on
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
that satisfies the same bound. Moreover, using the mean value inequality, the estimate holds true for
P_{t}^{N}\varphi
with the right-hand side being uniform in
N\geq 1
. Letting
N\to\infty
, the convergence part of Proposition
3.2
ensures

P_{t}^{N}(\varphi)\to P_{t}\varphi

pointwisely on
\mathcal{C}^{-\varepsilon}
hence the bound and the strong Feller property.

\square

5.  Ergodicity for Anderson
\Phi^{4}_{2}
model

In order to obtain exponential mixing from the strong Feller property, Tsatsoulis and Weber
[
24
]
proved a support theorem for the enhanced noise which implies a support result for the solution, following arguments by Chouk and Friz
[
5
]
. This argument relies strongly on a construction on the torus with the explicit eigenfunctions of the Laplacian. While they prove that
(0,a,0)
is in the support of their enhanced noise for any
a\geq 0
, we only proved that
(0,0,0)
is in the support of
\mathbf{z}
here, this is the content of Proposition
2.5
. We prove in the following that this is still enough to get exponential mixing and uniqueness of the invariant measure. Since the equation with
\mathbf{z}=0
relaxes to
0
exponentially fast, the following proposition states that this still holds if the enhanced noise is small in
L^{\infty}([0,T],\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa})^{3}
which happends with positive probability. In particular, the speed of relaxation is independent of the initial data as the coming down from infinity a priori bound is uniform in
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}
. See also Chapter
5
in Da Prato and Zabczyk
[
8
]
for more details on this.

Proposition 5.1 –

For any
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
T=T(\varepsilon)>0
of order
|\log(\varepsilon)|
such that

(5.1)

\|v(T\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1-\kappa}}\leq\varepsilon

for any
\kappa>0
and
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}
with positive probability.

Proof :

Let
\varepsilon>0
and
T>0
. We work conditionnaly to

(5.2)

\|\mathbf{z}\|_{L^{q}([0,1],(\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa})^{3})}+\sup_{t\in[1,T]}\|\mathbf{z}_{t}\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa})^{3}}+\|\mathbf{z}\|_{L^{q}([0,T],\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa})^{3}}\leq\varepsilon

which is an event of positive probability with Proposition
2.5
. Recall that
v
satisfies the equation

(5.3)

\partial_{t}v+\mathcal{H}v+v^{3}+v^{2}\mathbf{z}^{(1)}+v\mathbf{z}^{(2)}+\mathbf{z}^{(3)}=0

with
v(0)=u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}
. First, the coming down from infinity with Corollary
3.15
gives the bound

(5.4)

\|v(1)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}}\lesssim\|v(1)\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim 1

independant of
u_{0}
with
p\geq 1
large enough. Since the noise is bounded, the well-posedness result from Proposition
3.1
with
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{z}
gives

(5.5)

\sup_{t\in[2,T]}\|v(t)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}}\lesssim c

for any
\sigma<1
with a constant
c>0
. Testing the equation for
v
against
v
yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}\|v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\langle\mathcal{H}v,v\rangle-\|v\|_{L^{4}}^{4}-\langle v^{3},\mathbf{z}^{(1)}\rangle-\langle v^{2},\mathbf{z}^{(2)}\rangle-\langle v,\mathbf{z}^{(3)}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq-\lambda_{0}\|v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|v\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}}^{3}\|\mathbf{z}^{(1)}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}}+\|v\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}}^{2}\|\mathbf{z}^{(2)}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}}+\|v\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\sigma}}\|\mathbf{z}^{(3)}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}}

\displaystyle\leq-\lambda_{0}\|v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+c\varepsilon

which gives

(5.6)

\|v(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\|v(2)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}e^{-2\lambda_{0}(t-2)}+\frac{c\varepsilon}{\lambda_{0}}\big(1-e^{-2\lambda_{0}(t-2)}\big)

for
t\geq 2
with a Gröwall type argument. Taking
T=T(\varepsilon)>0
large enough, we get

(5.7)

\|v(T)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\varepsilon\big(1+\frac{c}{\lambda_{0}}\big)

which is arbitrary small as
\varepsilon>0
goes to zero. This completes the proof since

(5.8)

\displaystyle\|v(T)\|_{C^{1-\kappa+\kappa\theta-2\theta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|v(T)\|_{B_{\frac{2}{\theta},\frac{2}{\theta}}^{(1-\kappa)(1-\theta)}}

(5.9)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|v(T)\|_{L^{2}}^{\theta}\|v(T)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1-\kappa}}^{1-\theta}

(5.10)

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{\theta}{2}}

for any
\theta\in(0,1)
using interpolation and embbeding of Besov spaces.

\square

Remark 5.2 –

In the work
[
24
]
, Tsatsoulis and Weber consider general odd power
n
except for the support theorem which they only show in the case
n=3
. This is because they only prove the support theorem for the enhanced noise in the cubic case while they expect the result to be true for general power, see their remark
6.2
. Our approach covers their case as we only need that the support of the enhanced noise contains zero, which is straighforward to prove in their case.

Proposition 5.3 –

There exists
\lambda\in(0,1)
and
t_{\lambda}>0
such that

(5.11)

\|P_{t}^{*}\delta_{u_{0}}-P_{t}^{*}\delta_{\tilde{u}_{0}}\|_{\text{TV}}\leq 1-\lambda

for
t\geq t_{\lambda}
and
u_{0},\tilde{u}_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}
.

Proof :

Let
\kappa_{0}>\kappa>0
and consider

(5.12)

\mathcal{A}_{R}:=\{u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa_{0}}\ ;\ \|u_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}}\leq R\}

for any
R>0
wich is a compact of
\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa_{0}}
since the embedding
\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa_{0}}
is compact. By Corollary
4.12
, for every
a>0
there exists
r=r(a)>0
such that

(5.13)

\|P_{t}^{*}\delta_{u_{0}}-P_{t}^{*}\delta_{\tilde{u}_{0}}\|_{\text{TV}}\leq 1-a

for any
u_{0},\tilde{u}_{0}\in B_{\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}}(0,r)
the ball of radius
r
and center
0
in
\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}
. Proposition
5.1
gives the existence of
T=T(a)>0
such that

(5.14)

(P_{T}\mathds{1}_{B_{\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}}(0,r)})(u_{0})>0

for any
u_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{R}
. Since
\mathds{1}_{B_{\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}}(0,r)}
is a measurable function on
\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa_{0}}
, the strong Feller property implies that
u_{0}\mapsto(P_{T}\mathds{1}_{B_{\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}}(0,r)})(u_{0})
is a continuous function hence

(5.15)

\inf_{u_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{R}}(P_{T}\mathds{1}_{B_{\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}}(0,r)})(u_{0})\geq b>0

for a positive constant
b=b(a)>0
since
\mathcal{A}_{R}
is compact. Markov property gives

(5.16)

\displaystyle(P_{t}\Phi)(u_{0})-(P_{t}\Phi)(\tilde{u}_{0})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\big(P_{t-T}\phi)(u(T\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})\big)\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[\big(P_{t-T}\phi)(u(T\hskip 1.42271pt;\tilde{u}_{0})\big)\big]

(5.17)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa_{0}}}\big((P_{t}\Phi)(u_{1})-(P_{t}\Phi)(\tilde{u}_{1})\big)\mathbf{P}_{T}^{u_{0}}(\mathrm{d}u_{1})\otimes\mathbf{P}_{T}^{\tilde{u}_{0}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{u}_{1})

for
t\geq T
and
\Phi
any borelian function on
\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa_{0}}
where
\mathbf{P}_{T}^{u_{0}}(\mathrm{d}u_{1})
denotes the law of a couples of independent random variables distributed as
u(T\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})
. For
u_{0},\tilde{u}_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{R}
and
A=B_{\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}}(0,r)\times B_{\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}}(0,r)
, we get

(5.18)

\displaystyle\|P_{t}^{*}\delta_{u_{0}}-P_{t}^{*}\delta_{\tilde{u}_{0}}\|_{\text{TV}}

\displaystyle\leq(\mathbf{P}_{T}^{u_{0}}\otimes\mathbf{P}_{T}^{\tilde{u}_{0}})(A^{C})+(1-a)(\mathbf{P}_{T}^{u_{0}}\otimes\mathbf{P}_{T}^{\tilde{u}_{0}})(A)

(5.19)

\displaystyle\leq 1-a(\mathbf{P}_{T}^{u_{0}}\otimes\mathbf{P}_{T}^{\tilde{u}_{0}})(A)

(5.20)

\displaystyle\leq 1-ab^{2}

for
t\geq T
which is the claimed result. Taking
R>0
large enough, we have

(5.21)

\inf_{u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa_{0}}}\inf_{t\geq 1}\mathbb{P}(\|u(t\hskip 1.42271pt;u_{0})\|_{\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}}\leq R)>\frac{1}{2}

using Markov ineguality with the uniform bound from Corollary
4.2
uniform in
t\geq 1
and
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa_{0}}
. As above with
B=\mathcal{A}_{R}\times\mathcal{A}_{R}
, we get

(5.22)

\displaystyle\|P_{t}^{*}\delta_{u_{0}}-P_{t}^{*}\delta_{\tilde{u}_{0}}\|_{\text{TV}}

\displaystyle\leq(\mathbf{P}_{1}^{u_{0}}\otimes\mathbf{P}_{1}^{\tilde{u}_{0}})(B^{C})+(1-ab^{2})(\mathbf{P}_{1}^{u_{0}}\otimes\mathbf{P}_{1}^{\tilde{u}_{0}})(B)

(5.23)

\displaystyle\leq 1-ab^{2}(\mathbf{P}_{1}^{u_{0}}\otimes\mathbf{P}_{1}^{\tilde{u}_{0}})(B)

(5.24)

\displaystyle\leq 1-\frac{ab^{2}}{4}

for
t\geq 1+T
using that the two independent solutions belongs to
\mathcal{A}_{R}
with probability greater than
\frac{1}{4}
which completes the proof.

\square

We finally prove the main result of exponential convergence to a unique invariant measure. In particular, the measure is the Gibbs measure constructed directly in our previous work
[
11
]
.

Corollary 5.4 –

There exists a unique invariant measure
\mu
on
\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}
and we have

(5.25)

\|P_{t}^{*}\delta_{u_{0}}-\mu\|_{\text{TV}}\leq(1-\lambda)^{\lfloor\frac{t}{t_{\lambda}}\rfloor}\|\delta_{u_{0}}-\mu\|_{\text{TV}}

for any
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}
and
t\geq t_{\lambda}
.

Proof :

For any measure
\mu,\tilde{\mu}
on
\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}
, we have

(5.26)

\|P_{t}^{*}\mu-P_{t}^{*}\tilde{\mu}\|_{\text{TV}}=\frac{1}{2}\sup_{\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq 1}\int_{\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}\times\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}}|P_{t}\varphi(u_{0})-P_{t}\varphi(\tilde{u}_{0})|\mathbf{P}(\mathrm{d}u_{0},\mathrm{d}\tilde{u}_{0})

for any coupling
\mathbf{P}
of
\mu
and
\tilde{\mu}
and
t\geq t_{\lambda}
. Proposition
5.3
gives

(5.27)

\|P_{t}^{*}\mu-P_{t}^{*}\tilde{\mu}\|_{\text{TV}}\leq(1-\lambda)(1-\mathbf{P}(D))

with
D=\{(u_{0},u_{0});u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}\}
the diagonal of
\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}\times\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}
. Since the total variation distance can be characterized as

(5.28)

\|\mu-\tilde{\mu}\|_{\text{TV}}=\inf\big\{1-\mathbf{P}(D)\ ;\ \mathbf{P}\text{ any coupling of }\mu\text{ and }\tilde{\mu}\big\}

thus

(5.29)

\|P_{t}^{*}\mu-P_{t}^{*}\tilde{\mu}\|_{\text{TV}}\leq(1-\lambda)\|\mu-\tilde{\mu}\|_{\text{TV}}

which implies that the invariant measure is unique as two distinct invariant measure are singular, see
[
8
]
. Denoting as
\mu
the unique invariant measure, we get

(5.30)

\|P_{t}^{*}\delta_{u_{0}}-\mu\|_{\text{TV}}\leq(1-\lambda)\|P_{t-t_{\lambda}}^{*}\delta_{u_{0}}-\mu\|_{\text{TV}}

for any
u_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{-\kappa}
which completes the proof.

\square

Appendix A
The Anderson Hamiltonian

We gather here results on
\mathcal{H}
needed in this work, we refer to
[
3
,
19
]
and references therein for details, see also Section
4
in our previous work
[
11
]
. The condition for exponents to belongs to
(-1,1)
is due to the roughness of the noise
\xi
. First, we need the continuity of the Anderson heat semigroup in Hölder spaces.

Proposition A.1 –

For any
\alpha\in(-1,1)
, the semigroup
e^{-t\mathcal{H}}
is continuous from
\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}
to itself.

This is Proposition
4.1
in
[
11
]
, see also Proposition
4.6
in
[
3
]
.

Proposition A.2 –

For any
\alpha,\beta\in(-1,1)
such that
\alpha<\beta
, we have

(A.1)

\|e^{-t\mathcal{H}}u_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\beta}}\lesssim t^{-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}}\|u_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}}

for any
t\in(0,1)
.

This follows from Proposition
4.12
in
[
3
]
.

Proposition A.3 –

There exists a constant
c>0
such that

(A.2)

K_{t}(x,y)\lesssim\frac{1}{t}e^{-c\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{t}}

for any
t\in(0,1)
and
x,y\in\mathbb{T}^{2}
.

In the construction of the Anderson Hamiltonian, a crucial tool is the
\Gamma
map defined as the inverse of the map

(A.3)

\Phi(v)=v-v\mathbin{\lx@scalerel@obj{\hbox to8.9pt{\vbox to8.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.45pt\lower-4.45pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.88 0 C 5.88 3.25 3.25 5.88 0 5.88 C -3.25 5.88 -5.88 3.25 -5.88 0 C -5.88 -3.25 -3.25 -5.88 0 -5.88 C 3.25 -5.88 5.88 -3.25 5.88 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.125pt}{-1.75pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -4.32 -2.42)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}X_{>n}

where
X=\Delta^{-1}\xi\in\mathcal{C}^{1-\kappa}
for any
\kappa>0
. This map is not invertible in general, one can introduce a truncation
X_{>n}
as done in
[
15
]
such that the map

(A.4)

\Phi_{n}(v)=v-v\mathbin{\lx@scalerel@obj{\hbox to8.9pt{\vbox to8.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.45pt\lower-4.45pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.88 0 C 5.88 3.25 3.25 5.88 0 5.88 C -3.25 5.88 -5.88 3.25 -5.88 0 C -5.88 -3.25 -3.25 -5.88 0 -5.88 C 3.25 -5.88 5.88 -3.25 5.88 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.125pt}{-1.75pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -4.32 -2.42)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}X_{>n}

is a small perturbation of the identity thus invertible for
n
large enough depending on
\|X\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1-\kappa}}
, see Section
2.1.1
in
[
15
]
or Section
2.2
in
[
19
]
. In particular,
\Phi_{n}
and
\Gamma_{n}
converges to the identity as
n
goes to infinity hence continuity estimates are uniform with respect to
n
large enough.

Lemma A.4 –

There exists
n_{0}=n_{0}(\xi)>0
such that the maps
\Phi_{n}
and
\Gamma_{n}
are continuous on
L^{p}
for
p\geq 1
uniformly over
n\geq n_{0}
.

Recall that

(A.5)

\Gamma_{n}^{-1}\mathcal{H}\Gamma_{n}w=-\Delta w+\xi\mathbin{\lx@scalerel@obj{\hbox to9.14pt{\vbox to9.14pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.57158pt\lower-4.57158pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 6.05 0 C 6.05 3.34 3.34 6.05 0 6.05 C -3.34 6.05 -6.05 3.34 -6.05 0 C -6.05 -3.34 -3.34 -6.05 0 -6.05 C 3.34 -6.05 6.05 -3.34 6.05 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.125pt}{-1.74626pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -4.32 -2.42)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}w+R_{n}(w)

with
R_{n}
depending on the enhanced noise. The following result follows from the construction in
[
19
]
, see also Lemma
4.5
in
[
3
]
. Note that the roughest term
\xi\mathbin{\lx@scalerel@obj{\hbox to9.14pt{\vbox to9.14pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.57158pt\lower-4.57158pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{{}}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 6.05 0 C 6.05 3.34 3.34 6.05 0 6.05 C -3.34 6.05 -6.05 3.34 -6.05 0 C -6.05 -3.34 -3.34 -6.05 0 -6.05 C 3.34 -6.05 6.05 -3.34 6.05 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.125pt}{-1.74626pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -4.32 -2.42)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}w
is not included in
R_{n}
here thus the exponent
\kappa
in the result.

Lemma A.5 –

The remainder term is a bounded operator

R_{n}:B_{q,\infty}^{\kappa}\to B_{q,\infty}^{-\kappa^{\prime}}

for arbitrary
\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}>0
and
q\in[1,\infty]
.

Appendix B
Estimates in Besov spaces

A natural and convenient setting is given by the Besov space
B_{p,q}^{\alpha}
which can be defined using the Littlewood-Paley decomposition, see for example Bahouri, Chemin and Danchin’s book
[
1
]
. This decomposition can be stated as follows

u=\sum_{n\geq 0}\Delta_{n}u

with
\Delta_{n}u=\big(\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathds{1}_{|\cdot|\simeq 2^{n}}\mathcal{F}\big)u
, that is the projection of
u
in frequencies on an annulus of size
2^{n}
. It is defined by

\big(\Delta_{n}u\big)(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\chi_{n}(x-y)u(y)\mathrm{d}y:=2^{d(n-1)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\chi\big(2^{n-1}(x-y)\big)u(y)\mathrm{d}y

with
\chi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\text{supp}\ \widehat{\chi}\subset\{\frac{1}{2}\leq|z|\leq 2\}
for
n\geq 1
and

\big(\Delta_{0}u\big)(x):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\chi_{0}(x-y)u(y)\mathrm{d}y

with
\chi_{0}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\text{supp}\ \widehat{\chi}_{0}\subset\{|z|\leq 1\}
. We also denote
K=\widehat{\chi}
such that
\Delta_{n}u=\big(\mathcal{F}^{-1}K(2^{n}\cdot)\mathcal{F}\big)u
. Then the Besov space
B_{p,q}^{\alpha}
are distributions such that

\|u\|_{B_{p,q}^{\alpha}}:=\Big(\ \sum_{n\geq 0}2^{\alpha qn}\|\Delta_{n}u\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}^{q}\ \Big)^{\frac{1}{q}}<\infty

where the sum is understood to be a supremum if
q=\infty
. The particular case
p=q=2
corresponds to the Sobolev space
B_{2,2}^{\alpha}=H^{\alpha}
and for
p=q=\infty
with
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\backslash\mathbb{N}
, one gets the usual Hölder spaces
B_{\infty,\infty}^{\alpha}=\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}
. The following lemma gathers important properties such as embedding or product rule.

Lemma B.1 –

The Besov spaces
B^{s}_{p,q}
defined above satisfy the following properties.

(i)
For any
s\in\mathbb{R}
we have
B^{s}_{2,2}=H^{s}
, and more generally for any
2\leq p<\infty
and
\varepsilon>0
we have

\displaystyle\|f\|_{B^{s}_{p,\infty}}\lesssim\|f\|_{W^{s,p}}\lesssim\|f\|_{B^{s}_{p,2}}\lesssim\|f\|_{B^{s+\varepsilon}_{p,\infty}}.

(ii)
Let
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
1\leq p_{1}\leq p_{2}\leq\infty
and
q_{1},q_{2}\in[1,\infty]
. Set
r=s-2\big(\frac{1}{p_{1}}-\frac{1}{p_{2}}\big)
, then for any
f\in B^{s}_{p_{1},q}
, we have the embedding

\displaystyle\|f\|_{B^{r}_{p_{2},q}}\lesssim\|f\|_{B^{s}_{p_{1},q}}.

(iii)
Let
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\alpha+\beta>0
and
p_{1},p_{2},q_{1},q_{2}\in[1,\infty]
with

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}=\frac{1}{p_{1}}+\frac{1}{p_{2}}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{q_{1}}+\frac{1}{q_{2}}.

Then for any
f\in B^{\alpha}_{p_{1},q_{1}}
and
g\in B^{\beta}_{p_{2},q_{2}}
, we have
fg\in B^{\alpha\wedge\beta}_{p,q}
and the following holds.

•

If
\alpha\wedge\beta<0
, then

\displaystyle\|fg\|_{B^{\alpha\wedge\beta}_{p,q}}\lesssim\|f\|_{B^{\alpha}_{p_{1},q_{1}}}\|g\|_{B^{\beta}_{p_{2},q_{2}}}.

•

If
\alpha\wedge\beta>0
, then

\displaystyle\|fg\|_{B^{\alpha\wedge\beta}_{p,q}}\lesssim\|f\|_{B^{\alpha\wedge\beta}_{p_{1},q_{1}}}\|g\|_{B^{\alpha\wedge\beta}_{p_{2},q_{2}}}.

(iv)
Let
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
and
p,q\in[1,\infty]
. Then we have

(B.1)

\Big|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}f(x)g(x)\mathrm{d}x\Big|\lesssim\|f\|_{B_{p,q}^{\alpha}}\|g\|_{B_{p^{\prime},q^{\prime}}^{-\alpha}}

where
p^{\prime},q^{\prime}
are the respective conjugate exponent of
p,q
.

From
[
17
]
, we gather the two following most useful estimates. The following lemma is a useful interpolation estimate.

Lemma B.2 –

Let
\alpha_{0},\alpha_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
,
p_{0},p_{1},q_{0},q_{1}\in[1,+\infty]
and set

\alpha=\theta\alpha_{1}+(1-\theta)\alpha_{2}\qquad\frac{1}{p}=\frac{\theta}{p_{1}}+\frac{1-\theta}{p_{2}}\qquad\frac{1}{q}=\frac{\theta}{q_{1}}+\frac{1-\theta}{q_{2}}

for
\theta\in[0,1]
. Then

\|f\|_{B^{\alpha}_{p,q}}\lesssim\|f\|_{B^{\alpha_{1}}_{p_{1},q_{1}}}^{\theta}\|f\|_{B^{\alpha_{2}}_{p_{2},q_{2}}}^{1-\theta}.

This product rule in mixed Besov and Lebesgue spaces is useful for energy estimate.

Lemma B.3 –

Let
\alpha>0
,
r\in\mathbb{N}
and
p_{1},p_{2},q\in[1,+\infty]
. Set

\frac{1}{p}=\frac{1}{p_{1}}+\frac{1}{p_{2}}

then

\|f^{r+1}\|_{B^{\alpha}_{p,q}}\lesssim\|f^{r}\|_{L^{p_{1}}}\|f\|_{B^{\alpha}_{p_{2},q}}.

We will also make use of the following fractional Leibniz rule in
L^{2}
-based Sobolev spaces.

Lemma B.4 –

Let
r,s<1
with
r+s>0
, and
t=r+s-1
. Then there exists
C>0
such that for any
u\in H^{s}
and
v\in H^{r}
, we have the fractional Leibniz rule

\|uv\|_{H^{t}}\leq C\|u\|_{H^{s}}\|v\|_{H^{r}}.

Proof :

The corresponding estimate in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
is proved in
[
23
, Corollary 2.1]
. Then the one on the torus follows from the one in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
through the use of a finite partition of unity.

\square
